# Profile JAX training

## Scope
Ingestion sources:
- XPlane protobufs inside Levanter profile directories (source of truth):
  - `plugins/profile/<timestamp>/*.xplane.pb`
  - explicit local `*.xplane.pb` files via `--xplane-file`
- xprof aggregate tables exported from the same XPlane protobuf when the
  optional `xprof` package is available: step overview timing, kernel stats,
  collective breakdowns, xprof bottleneck statements.
- Perfetto trace JSON as an explicit/fallback source for older profiles:
  - `plugins/profile/<timestamp>/perfetto_trace.json.gz`
  - `plugins/profile/<timestamp>/*.trace.json.gz`

Prefer XPlane protobuf for new work. Perfetto trace JSON commonly hits the trace
event cap; XPlane contains the uncapped timeline events needed for named-scope
regions, pre-op gaps, gap context, process/thread metadata, and xprof aggregate
tables. Use `--trace-file` only for a specific Perfetto JSON trace or an older
profile with no XPlane protobuf.

## Capture Profiles
Use Levanter profiler flags so profiles land under
`<trainer.log_dir>/<run_id>/profiler`. Remote Marin runs also upload to
`MARIN_PREFIX` TTL storage and print an XProf link:

```bash
uv run ... \
  --trainer.profiler.enabled true \
  --trainer.profiler.start_step 5 \
  --trainer.profiler.num_steps 10 \
  --trainer.profiler.upload.ttl_days 30
```

For profiles where xprof/HLO protobuf tables matter, enable JAX profile options
through the Levanter profiler config:

```bash
uv run ... \
  --trainer.profiler.enabled true \
  --trainer.profiler.start_step 5 \
  --trainer.profiler.num_steps 5 \
  --trainer.profiler.profile_options.host_tracer_level 1 \
  --trainer.profiler.profile_options.python_tracer_level 0 \
  --trainer.profiler.profile_options.device_tracer_level 0 \
  --trainer.profiler.profile_options.enable_hlo_proto true
```

HLO metadata increases artifact size, so keep these profile windows short. The
`XProf profile:` link appears after upload. Set
`--trainer.profiler.upload.enabled false` for local-only capture. Do not copy
profiles to another GCS region for inspection.

Known-good TensorBoard scope recipe from CoreWeave Grug MoE profiling:
`trainer.profiler.enabled=true`, `trainer.profiler.start_step=3`,
`trainer.profiler.num_steps=2`, `trainer.profiler.perfetto_link=false`,
`trainer.profiler.profile_options.host_tracer_level=1`,
`trainer.profiler.profile_options.python_tracer_level=0`, and
`trainer.profiler.profile_options.enable_hlo_proto=true` preserved useful
`jax.named_scope` / `named_call` regions in TensorBoard for
`GM2560-MAY-120S4096-W2048-B8-R1-E8M1-FA4PROFILE-S3B-N1-cw-20260617-2353`.
Leave `device_tracer_level` unset unless device timelines are specifically
needed; this profile retained useful hierarchical host/XLA metadata without it.

On GPU, command buffers can collapse or suppress the visible name stack in
TensorBoard/Perfetto. For profile-readability runs, disable command buffers:

```bash
export XLA_FLAGS="${XLA_FLAGS:-} --xla_gpu_enable_command_buffer=''"
```

This hurts performance, so use it only when the goal is semantic trace
attribution; leave it out of throughput comparisons unless command-buffer
behavior is the axis being tested.

For GPU throughput runs, keep profile-readability flags separate from XLA code
generation and scheduling flags. Start from JAX's GPU performance guide,
especially the code generation flags section:
<https://docs.jax.dev/en/latest/gpu_performance_tips.html#code-generation-flags>.
The exact set of useful XLA flags is `jaxlib`-version dependent, so record the
full `XLA_FLAGS` value with each profile or W&B run.

For better profile readability, use `haliax.jax_utils.named_call` and
`jax.named_scope` liberally in model code; these names flow into trace
annotations and make region-level summaries far more actionable.

Reference:
- `lib/levanter/docs/Performance-Guide.md`
- `.agents/skills/add-pallas-kernel/SKILL.md`
- JAX GPU performance tips:
  <https://docs.jax.dev/en/latest/gpu_performance_tips.html>

## Ingest to Structured Summary
Use `/tmp` for ephemeral downloads. Use `scratch/` only when the working tree
must retain an uncommitted analysis artifact.

```bash
# /tmp (ephemeral)
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py summarize \
  --run-target marin-community/marin/<run_id> \
  --download-root /tmp/marin-profiles \
  --breakdown-mode exclusive_global \
  --output /tmp/profile_summary.json

```

### Option A: From a W&B artifact reference

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py summarize \
  --artifact marin-community/marin/run-grug-125m-profile-apples-pallas_tpu-20260217-225239-055ab2-profiler:v0 \
  --download-root /tmp/marin-profiles \
  --output /tmp/profile_summary.json
```

`--run-target` accepts: a bare run id (requires `--entity` and `--project`),
`entity/project/run_id`, or a full W&B run URL. The profiler directory is
resolved from `trainer.log_dir` in the run config.

### Option B: From a local artifact directory

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py summarize \
  --profile-dir /path/to/profiler_dir \
  --output /tmp/profile_summary.json
```

If the directory contains `*.xplane.pb`, `--profile-dir` uses the XPlane path
automatically. When both `*.xplane.pb` and Perfetto trace JSON are present,
`--profile-dir` reads the XPlane protobuf by default (Perfetto exports are often
capped). Use `--trace-file` to force a specific Perfetto JSON file.

### Option C: From a specific trace file

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py summarize \
  --trace-file /path/to/perfetto_trace.json.gz \
  --output /tmp/profile_summary.json
```

### Option D: From a specific XPlane protobuf

Direct XPlane timeline parsing uses `protobuf` and does not require
TensorFlow-generated `xplane_pb2` modules. If `xprof` is installed, ingestion
also exports compact xprof table JSON and augments the timeline summary with
aggregate step, kernel, collective, and bottleneck evidence.

```bash
uv run --with xprof --with protobuf python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py summarize \
  --xplane-file /path/to/profile.xplane.pb \
  --xplane-output-dir /tmp/profile_xprof_tables \
  --xplane-count-trace-events \
  --output /tmp/profile_summary.json
```

Without `--xplane-output-dir` the command still parses XPlane timeline events
directly. Add `--with xprof` for xprof aggregate table augmentation; add
`--xplane-output-dir` to preserve the exported table JSON (this flag requires
the optional `xprof` package).

XPlane summaries expose hierarchical named-scope regions, pre-op gaps, gap
region context, process/thread/timeline event metadata, step timing (when step
markers or xprof overview rows exist), xprof bottleneck statements, kernel
stats, collective breakdowns, and optimization candidates.

Summary version tag: `profile_summary.v1`

Generate a deterministic markdown root-cause report:

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py report \
  --summary /tmp/profile_summary.json \
  --output /tmp/profile_report.md
```

Trace quality checks are surfaced in `trace_overview`:
- `suspected_truncation`: `true` when event counts match a known export cap.
- `quality_warnings`: warnings to treat hotspot/gap attribution with caution.

## Query the summary

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py query \
  --summary /tmp/profile_summary.json \
  --question "<top ops, compute vs communication, gap, region, or op context>"
```

Query top exclusive-time ops, compute/communication balance and collectives,
specific pre-op gaps, hierarchical regions, noisy-op context, and suggested
optimizations.

Useful query forms include:

- `What are the top 10 ops by exclusive time?`
- `Is comm or compute dominating? Which collective is worst?`
- `gap before _linear_softmax_cross_entropy_loss_bwd_pallas_mosaic_tpu_combined.1`
- `show hierarchical regions`
- `show context for op copy.564`
- `What should we try next?`

Pre-op gap attribution is marker-aware:
- `gap_before_ops[].payload_op`: op where useful work starts after the idle period.
- `gap_before_ops[].marker_op`: first op observed after the gap (often
  lightweight setup like `iota.*`).

## Optimization Workflow
Use a strict workflow:

1. **Measure**: generate `before.json`.
2. **Change**: apply one bounded patch/config tweak.
3. **Re-measure**: generate `after.json`.
4. **Compare**:

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py compare \
  --before /tmp/profile_before.json \
  --after /tmp/profile_after.json \
  --strict-provenance
```

5. **Track** (thresholded pass/warn/fail + history):

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py track \
  --before /tmp/profile_before.json \
  --after /tmp/profile_after.json \
  --label "pallas-kernel-attempt-3" \
  --history /tmp/profile_regression_history.jsonl
```

6. **History summary** (regression trend tracking):

```bash
uv run python lib/marin/tools/profile_summary.py history \
  --history /tmp/profile_regression_history.jsonl
```

    68	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    69	
    70	HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV = {
    71	    "LD_PRELOAD": "libjemalloc.so.2",
    72	    "MALLOC_CONF": "background_thread:true,dirty_decay_ms:0,muzzy_decay_ms:0,narenas:2",
    73	    "JAX_ENABLE_PGLE": "false",
    74	    "XLA_PJRT_GPU_HOST_MEMORY_LIMIT_GB": "192",
    75	    "XLA_PYTHON_CLIENT_ALLOCATOR": "cuda_async",
    76	    # `cuda_async` reads this fraction as the mempool release threshold, and PJRT sizes the
    77	    # collective pool at the remaining `(1 - fraction) x 184.3 GiB`. 0.75 sets a 138.2 GiB
    78	    # threshold and leaves ~46 GiB for collectives, above the ~28.5 GiB NCCL, cuBLAS, and the
    79	    # CUDA context hold outside the allocator. A higher fraction strands memory the step never
    80	    # uses, because the startup preallocation probe commits the whole threshold at once: 0.83
    81	    # pins 153.0 GiB in the pool and leaves under 3 GiB free on the device, so the arena
    82	    # allocation below has no room to remap into.
    83	    "XLA_PYTHON_CLIENT_MEM_FRACTION": "0.75",
    84	}
    85	XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG = "--xla_gpu_experimental_parallel_collective_overlap_limit"
    86	DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 4
    87	DEFAULT_DROPLESS_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION: MoeImplementation = "sonic_cute"
    88	# Full inline norm watch failed with overlap 4. Overlap 1 completed the selected full-watch gate.
    89	INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 1
    90	# The ragged transport wants the opposite scheduling posture from the fixed and pooled ones. Its
    91	# dispatch and combine form one long dependent chain, so admitting several concurrent collectives
    92	# only contends for the SMs the transport itself needs.
    93	RAGGED_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 1
    94	# The layer-carry offload, the latency-hiding scheduler, and an overlap limit above 1 corrupt
    95	# training together, and no pair of the three does: the reloaded residual races its consumer in
    96	# backward, so a restored run reports a first-step loss near 9.5 instead of its checkpoint's
    97	# (#8317). Overlap 4 gained 0.07 MFU, so the offload forces this value rather than defaulting it.
    98	OFFLOAD_CARRY_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 1
    99	RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION = "ragged_all_to_all"
   100	# TODO(https://github.com/marin-community/marin/issues/5675): Re-enable XLA GPU
   101	# command buffers after the CUDA graph failure is fixed.
   102	XLA_DISABLE_GPU_COMMAND_BUFFER_FLAG = "--xla_gpu_enable_command_buffer="
   103	_RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAG_NAMES = frozenset(flag.partition("=")[0] for flag in RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAGS)
   104	PJRT_DISTRIBUTION = "jax-cuda13-pjrt"
   105	_FP32_POLICY = jmp.get_policy("params=float32,compute=float32,output=float32")
   106	
   107	
   108	class WatchMode(StrEnum):
   109	    """Where a watched training step computes gradient and parameter statistics."""
   110	
   111	    INLINE = "inline"
   112	    DIAGNOSTIC = "diagnostic"
   113	
   114	
   115	class MasterParamMode(StrEnum):
   116	    """Where the authoritative fp32 weights live.
   117	
   118	    DEVICE keeps them as the device params themselves, with no separate master copy;
   119	    FP32_PINNED_HOST keeps a pinned-host fp32 master while the device params are its bf16 cast.
   120	    """
   121	
   122	    DEVICE = "device"
   123	    FP32_PINNED_HOST = "fp32_pinned_host"
   124	
   125	
   126	class TrainingDataMode(StrEnum):
   127	    """Source of training batches."""
   128	
   129	    MIXTURE = "mixture"
   130	    SYNTHETIC = "synthetic"
   131	
   132	
   133	def restore_template_from(state):
   134	    """ShapeDtypeStructs carrying each leaf's concrete sharding, releasing the leaves.
   135	
   136	    `jax.eval_shape` drops the `pinned_host` memory kind that `initial_state` puts on
   137	    offloaded optimizer state, which is most of a hero checkpoint. Reading the sharding off a
   138	    built state keeps it.
   139	    """
   140	    template = jax.tree.map(
   141	        lambda leaf: (
   142	            jax.ShapeDtypeStruct(leaf.shape, leaf.dtype, sharding=leaf.sharding) if isinstance(leaf, jax.Array) else leaf
   143	        ),
   144	        state,
   145	    )
   146	    jax.tree.map(lambda leaf: leaf.delete() if isinstance(leaf, jax.Array) else None, state)
   147	    gc.collect()
   148	    return template
   149	
   150	
   151	def checkpoint_stores_master(candidate: str) -> bool:
   152	    """Whether checkpoint ``candidate``'s manifest lists fp32 master parameters.
   153	
   154	    A missing manifest raises ``FileNotFoundError``, which restore treats like any other
   155	    unreadable candidate.
   156	    """
   157	    manifest = read_manifest(candidate)
   158	    if manifest is None:
   159	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"{candidate} has no manifest.json, so its layout cannot be read")
   160	    markers = (MASTER_PARAMS_KEY, f"{LEGACY_STATE_KEY}/{MASTER_PARAMS_KEY}")
   161	    return any(path == marker or path.startswith(marker + "/") for path in manifest.array_paths for marker in markers)
   162	
   163	
   164	def template_for_candidate_layout(
   165	    state: "GrugTrainState", candidate: str, run_mode: MasterParamMode
   166	) -> "GrugTrainState":
   167	    """Pick the template restore reads checkpoint ``candidate`` with.
   168	
   169	    Restore reads only the leaves the template names and takes each dtype from storage, checking
   170	    neither against the checkpoint, so reading a master-bearing checkpoint with the run's own
   171	    master-less template would silently return the bf16 compute copy. When the layouts match the
   172	    template is ``state`` itself. A master-bearing checkpoint read by a master-less run migrates
   173	    in process: the run's device fp32 ``params`` template is presented under ``master_params``, so
   174	    the checkpoint's authoritative fp32 master loads directly into it and the bf16 copy goes
   175	    unread; ``take_master_as_params`` then moves it back, and the next save writes the new layout.
   176	    """
   177	    has_master = checkpoint_stores_master(candidate)
   178	    if has_master == (run_mode != MasterParamMode.DEVICE):
   179	        return state
   180	    if not has_master:
   181	        raise ValueError(
   182	            f"checkpoint {candidate} stores no master parameters, but this run trains with {run_mode}. "
   183	            "Synthesizing a master from stored weights is not a conversion this supports."
   184	        )
   185	    logger.info("Checkpoint %s stores a pinned-host fp32 master; restoring it as the parameters.", candidate)
   186	    return dataclasses.replace(state, params=None, master_params=state.params)
   187	
   188	
   189	def take_master_as_params(state: "GrugTrainState") -> "GrugTrainState":
   190	    """Move a master restored through ``template_for_candidate_layout`` into ``params``."""
   191	    if state.master_params is None:
   192	        return state
   193	    return dataclasses.replace(state, params=state.master_params, master_params=None)
   194	
   195	
   196	def _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults(
   197	    *,
   198	    inline_watch_enabled: bool,
   199	    moe_implementation: MoeImplementation | None,
   200	    remat_mode: RematMode,
   201	    processes_per_task: int = 1,
   202	) -> None:
   203	    env_defaults = dict(HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV)
   204	    if processes_per_task > 1:
   205	        # With one process per GPU, the per-process CUPTI sessions collide with each
   206	        # other and with CoreWeave's DCGM, so PGLE cannot profile and its recompile
   207	        # machinery only adds failure modes. Default it off; an explicit env wins.
   208	        env_defaults["JAX_ENABLE_PGLE"] = "false"
   209	    for name, value in env_defaults.items():
   210	        os.environ.setdefault(name, value)
   211	    xla_flags = os.environ.get("XLA_FLAGS", "").split()
   212	    ragged = moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION
   213	    if ragged:
   214	        overlap_limit = RAGGED_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   215	    elif inline_watch_enabled:
   216	        overlap_limit = INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   217	    else:
   218	        overlap_limit = DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   219	    # The scheduler's longer buffer live ranges fit on the ragged transport only once the layer
   220	    # carry leaves HBM. Without that offload its first-step NCCL allocations fail.
   221	    latency_hiding = not ragged or remat_mode == "offload_carry"
   222	    flag_defaults = (
   223	        f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={overlap_limit}",
   224	        f"--xla_gpu_enable_latency_hiding_scheduler={'true' if latency_hiding else 'false'}",
   225	        # The scheduler sizes the single `jit_train_step` temp arena against this percentage of
   226	        # its memory budget, roughly `133.6 GiB x percentage`. The pool holds 138.2 GiB and
   227	        # persistent state occupies 18.1 GiB of it, so an arena above 120.2 GiB cannot be served
   228	        # from pool free space and forces a fresh mapping against the ~17 GiB of physical memory
   229	        # outside the pool. The default 95 asks for 125.7 GiB and fails that way. 85 sizes the
   230	        # arena at 113.6 GiB, leaving enough slack for per-node variation in fragmentation. A
   231	        # lower percentage costs throughput, because a smaller arena makes `HloRematerialization`
   232	        # recompute more of the step.
   233	        "--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85",
   234	        XLA_DISABLE_GPU_COMMAND_BUFFER_FLAG,
   235	    )
   236	    explicit_names = {flag.partition("=")[0] for flag in xla_flags}
   237	    xla_flags.extend(flag for flag in flag_defaults if flag.partition("=")[0] not in explicit_names)
   238	    if remat_mode == "offload_carry":
   239	        # Not overridable, unlike the defaults above, including the scheduler flag it pairs with.
   240	        # Both flags can be inherited from another recipe, but only this one fails silently: a
   241	        # wrong overlap limit corrupts training, while an inherited scheduler setting either
   242	        # aborts in NCCL's first-step allocation and names itself, or runs the slower posture.
   243	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] != XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG]
   244	        xla_flags.append(f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={OFFLOAD_CARRY_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT}")
   245	    if ragged:
   246	        # Unlike the defaults above, these are not overridable. Selecting the host-launched
   247	        # one-shot kernel needs both flags cleared together plus a splits-per-peer count this
   248	        # branch no longer carries, so honoring a partial override would run a configuration
   249	        # nothing here measures. Drop any conflicting entry rather than relying on which
   250	        # occurrence XLA's parser keeps.
   251	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] not in _RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAG_NAMES]
   252	        xla_flags.extend(RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAGS)
   253	    os.environ["XLA_FLAGS"] = " ".join(xla_flags)
   254	
   255	
   256	def verify_ragged_pjrt() -> None:
   257	    """Raise unless this process runs Marin's patched GPU PJRT plugin."""
   258	    try:
   259	        installed = importlib.metadata.version(PJRT_DISTRIBUTION)
   260	    except importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError as missing:

---
name: consult-echo
description: Search or cite Echo when Marin's prior-work policy applies, another selected workflow requires it, or the user explicitly asks; do not substitute it for local code search.
---

# Consult Echo

Search Echo before rediscovering prior work. Cite canonical Echo, GitHub, or
Discord URLs for the evidence you use.

## Search

Use a natural-language federated search:

```bash
uv run infra/echo/cli.py search "how do I diagnose a stalled TPU collective" --limit 10
```

Search covers `wiki`, `file`, `pr`, and `issue` by default. Repeat `--domain` to
select a subset.

File search infers the configured Marin-community repository from the current
Git checkout, including ordinary contributor forks. Pass `--repository
<owner/repo>` to choose one configured repository or `--repository all` to
search all six. The CLI prints the resolved file scope once. Searches that omit
the file domain work outside a Git checkout.

Discord is excluded by default because messages may be noisy or untrusted. Add
`--domain discord` only when discussion history is relevant, and open the
canonical URL when surrounding thread context matters.

Fetch a result's full detail with its printed ID:

```bash
uv run infra/echo/cli.py get <domain:id>
```

Grade evaluated results with the exact query and printed keys:

```bash
uv run infra/echo/cli.py feedback --query "stalled TPU collective" \
  --grade wiki:730=0 --grade file:731=10 \
  <<< "The file result answered the task; wiki results were off-topic."
```

Use 0 for irrelevant and 10 for directly useful. Grade only evaluated results
and always explain overall quality on stdin, including for an empty result set.

Use `grep` for exact strings in GitHub or Discord activity, narrowing with
`--source` or `--kind` when needed:

```bash
uv run infra/echo/cli.py grep "FAILED_PRECONDITION" --source github
```

Use `rg` for exact identifiers in the current checkout. Echo's file index
follows the periodically refreshed GitHub head, so it does not contain
branch-only or uncommitted files. Use `wiki search --tag <tag>` when tags, rather
than federated relevance, define the desired synthesis. Authentication reuses
the shared Marin login via `iris login`; see
[Echo setup and access](../../../infra/echo/README.md).

Run this search sequence before adding or editing a wiki note.

## Choose the durable home

Every incident gets a standalone Echo record through `write-ops-log`, linked
from its associated PR or issue. Also use the narrowest reusable home when the
incident or other work changes durable guidance:

- Update `OPS.md` for a recurring operational procedure, diagnostic workflow,
  or guardrail in one subsystem.
- Update `docs/` for reusable product or user guidance that belongs with the
  repository.
- Edit an existing Echo wiki note for a cross-cutting pattern or synthesis that
  has no single repository home.
- Add a wiki note only when the searches find no near-duplicate.

Create one incident entry per incident and edit it while the same incident
continues. Keep raw logs and diffs in their source systems. Edit a near-duplicate
synthesis instead of adding another.

## Write or revise a wiki note

Write the shortest entry that changes a future action:

- Lead with an actionable value: a decision, command, diagnostic discriminator,
  guardrail, or recovery step.
- Include only the facts needed to support or apply that action. Link to the
  canonical PR, issue, dashboard, log bundle, or report for chronology and raw
  evidence instead of copying it into Echo.
- Put the action in the title, `use_when`, or opening paragraph.
- Keep chronology without a reusable decision in an issue, logbook, or source
  artifact.

Write an Open Knowledge Format document:

```markdown
---
type: wiki-note
title: Stalled TPU collectives can have several low-power causes
use_when: when a distributed TPU job stalls before the first optimizer update
tags:
  - ops
  - debugging
  - iris
---

State the reusable pattern, how to distinguish its causes, and the evidence URLs.
```

Use lowercase kebab-case tags. Keep the title concrete and make `use_when`
describe the future search trigger. Make the body stand alone. Cite canonical
URLs inline. Concision still requires enough context to apply the guidance
safely; do not omit a precondition, destructive-action warning, or material
caveat.

Create a note:

```bash
uv run infra/echo/cli.py wiki add --file note.md
```

Revise the closest existing note:

```bash
uv run infra/echo/cli.py wiki show <id> > note.md
# Edit note.md.
uv run infra/echo/cli.py wiki edit <id> --file note.md
```

Both write commands print the canonical Echo URL. Return and cite that URL
after every add or edit.

   105	else identical to control; env-only (no commit), recorded in arms.tsv extra
   106	column. Mechanism: raises the scheduler/remat temp-arena cap from 113.6 GiB
   107	(85) to ~120.2 GiB (90, at the documented pool-free-space bound); the 85
   108	calibration predates the carry offload (peak now ~116.5 GiB, was ~138) and
   109	train.py's own comment says the smaller arena makes HloRematerialization
   110	recompute more of the step.
   111	
   112	Draws: one T at slop90; if delta > bar candidate, a second T draw; controls
   113	bracket from iteration-0 draws plus a fresh control after (the keep rule's
   114	>=2C/>=2T). Ladder: OOM at 90 -> 88; green+gain at 90 -> consider 92 with the
   115	explicit caveat that arena 122.9 GiB exceeds the documented 120.2 bound and
   116	must survive a fresh-mapping against ~17 GiB physical outside the pool.
   117	
   118	Engagement check: memory/peak_gib (scored as peak_gib_max) must RISE vs
   119	control. MFU moved but peak did not -> not engaged, inspect task-log XLA_FLAGS
   120	echo before concluding anything. Failure modes: named first-step NCCL alloc
   121	abort or BFC OOM (cheap crash, ladder down).
   122	
   123	Confounds pre-declared: (a) remat disengaging when peak crosses the allocator
   124	boundary is the DESIRED mechanism here, not an artifact (#8054's trap inverted)
   125	— but drops and the paired loss series must stay clean; (b) cuda_async
   126	MEM_FRACTION is a RELEASE threshold (#8490): if the higher peak crosses it,
   127	the pool unmaps every step and the arm gets slower with high step-time
   128	variance — watch mfu_stdev, and treat a high-variance slowdown as
   129	threshold-crossing, not as "slop raise is bad".
   130	
   131	## H6 review dispositions (fable + codex, 2026-08-29; transcripts: review-codex-h6.txt)
   132	
   133	Both reviewers verified the flag path end-to-end (arm.sh -> coordinator ->
   134	XLA_ forwarding -> explicit-wins dedupe in _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults ->
   135	exactly one slop token =90 reaches the task; last-occurrence-wins in XLA's
   136	parser if ever duplicated). Both BLOCKED the spec as written. Revised spec:
   137	
   138	- ENGAGEMENT REDEFINED (codex blocker 1 / fable F2): slop sets a scheduler
   139	  and remat LIMIT, not an allocation; peak_bytes_in_use can stay flat under
   140	  genuine engagement and can rise for unrelated reasons (rank-0 lifetime
   141	  high-water incl. restore/migration transients). New scheme:
   142	  (1) config engagement — the flag present exactly once in the task env
   143	  (verified by local pre-flight of _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults + task
   144	  log); (2) mechanism evidence — compile-time remat/scheduler logs:
   145	  dispatch.py now forwards TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL/TF_CPP_VMODULE and every arm
   146	  runs TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL=0 TF_CPP_VMODULE=hlo_rematerialization=1, so
   147	  control and treatment logs carry the LHS limit line and remat stats.
   148	  peak_gib demoted to supporting telemetry.
   149	- OUTCOME SPACE PRE-DECLARED (fable F2a): {flag confirmed, remat/LHS logs
   150	  unchanged, peak flat, MFU flat} = DEAD-ON-THIS-STACK (a legitimate
   151	  conclusion, not instrumentation failure). BINDING PRE-CHECK: iteration-0
   152	  control logs are read FIRST — if control shows zero remat and LHS usage
   153	  well under the 113.6 limit, H6 closes as not-binding WITHOUT a treatment
   154	  arm.
   155	- MECHANISM LANGUAGE CORRECTED (fable F4): conclusions say "limit raise"
   156	  (feeds both LHS and HloRematerialization), not specifically "less remat";
   157	  post-offload actual temps (~98 GiB) sit far under both 113.6 and 120.2,
   158	  so an OOM at 90 is UNLIKELY and the 92-rung "exceeds the bound" caveat is
   159	  probably vacuous; any OOM gets diagnosed from the failed allocation size.
   160	- TREATMENT RECORDED (codex 2 / fable F1): arms.tsv gained an xla_flags
   161	  column (backfilled for mfl-ctrl-a), the submit echo prints it, and arm.sh
   162	  refuses a nonempty ARM_XLA_FLAGS without TREATMENT=1 — a leaked env var
   163	  can no longer contaminate a control silently.
   164	- FAILURE CLASSIFICATION (codex 5 / fable F3): any H6-arm failure requires a
   165	  task-log read before classification — BFC/CUDA/NCCL alloc signature ->
   166	  ladder (88); anything else -> invalid/resubmit. A compile-ceiling kill on
   167	  a treatment draw is checked against the control's compile time before
   168	  being read as anything; if the treatment legitimately compiles slower,
   169	  resubmit at interactive priority with a raised ceiling (production stays
   170	  under 1h).
   171	- FIDELITY TOLERANCE (codex 3 / fable F5): DESIGN now calibrates the
   172	  pointwise loss null band and a drops tolerance from iteration-0
   173	  control-control pairs; gate on median + calibrated max; small exceedance
   174	  -> extra draw/investigation, not auto-kill.
   175	- Verified fine by reviewers: no duplicate-flag hazard; per-RID compile
   176	  cache isolates the 85-compiled executable; max_retries_failure=0 means no
   177	  silent in-job retry; overlap-limit stays force-pinned under the
   178	  treatment; MEM_FRACTION release-threshold confound pre-declaration sound.
   179

   282	## 2026-08-29 ~09:10Z: H9/H10 mechanism diagnosis + treatment specs (agent investigation)
   283	
   284	H9 copies IDENTIFIED by exact size match: the ragged-transport latent-dim
   285	working buffers, not the layer carry — [TK=524288, 3072] bf16 = 3.221 GB
   286	(sorted_x/returned) and [C=301466, 3072] bf16 = 1.852 GB (per-chunk
   287	dispatch), 48 launches = layers, 9 ops = 3 copy sites x 3 loop bodies.
   288	Root cause: ragged-all-to-all is in-place (result aliases the output-init
   289	operand); the loop-invariant-hoisted zeros inits and the returned
   290	double-use force CopyInsertion to copy every iteration.
   291	
   292	- T-H9-1 (arm next): ARM_XLA_FLAGS --xla_gpu_async_copy_min_bytes=1000000000
   293	  -> GpuCopyAsyncWrapper turns the nine >=1GB D2D copies into
   294	  copy-start/done on their own stream under the ASYNC-COMPUTE resource
   295	  (limit 2), NOT the pinned collective overlap limit (verified separate
   296	  branches in gpu_latency_hiding_scheduler.cc). Predicted +0.4-0.9 MFU.
   297	  Engagement: profile tail — copy.7xx leaves the compute stream,
   298	  compute-stream MemcpyD2D busy -> ~0. Watch peak_gib and the
   299	  remat-count line (longer live ranges can eat H6's budget).
   300	- T-H10-1 (after H9-T1, separate arm): --xla_gpu_enable_pipelined_host_offloading=true
   301	  -> CollectivePipeliner rotates the carry writeback into iteration i+1
   302	  and the reload one iteration ahead (verified pass wiring at
   303	  gpu_compiler.cc:1213-1290; predicates match the jax scan-offload
   304	  pattern). Predicted +0.5-0.8 MFU. CORRECTNESS-SENSITIVE: changes reload
   305	  timing in the same machinery as the #8317 offload race (overlap limit
   306	  stays force-pinned at 1 — arms cannot override it). Hard gate: scored
   307	  loss series within the C-C null band (max ~1e-4); any excursion = the
   308	  race, not noise. Engagement: exposed offload-copy time -> ~0 in a
   309	  profile tail, peak +~1.6 GB.
   310	- DO NOT STACK the two in one arm (shared async-compute limit 2; joint
   311	  attribution lost). If both keep, back-ablate per the re-ablation rule;
   312	  --xla_gpu_experimental_parallel_async_compute_limit=4 is a stacking
   313	  refinement only.
     1	# Campaign: ragged EP hero MFU loop (loop-260828-8753mfu)
     2	
     3	Goal: raise hero MFU beyond the #8753 stack's frontier without fidelity loss
     4	(no added quantization; loss trajectory equivalent or better) and without
     5	breaking hero-checkpoint compatibility (every keep must hot-swap into the live
     6	hero run; checkpoint migration OK, restore from hero checkpoints must work).
     7	
     8	Cost model (user, 2026-08-29): rack capacity is use-it-or-lose-it — failed or
     9	extra arms are cheap (stay courteous to other rack users). The expensive
    10	failure is a WRONG CONCLUSION: being sent in a bad direction, or prematurely
    11	closing a promising one, because a flawed experiment misled the loop. Review
    12	cycles and arm design optimize for inferential validity first, rack frugality
    13	second. Concretely: every treatment arm pre-declares an ENGAGEMENT CHECK
    14	(evidence the lever actually engaged — a kernel name in a profile, an echoed
    15	flag, an HLO property, or a metric that cannot move unless the lever did);
    16	"no effect" results are only recorded as dead after engagement is confirmed;
    17	near-threshold results get more draws rather than a coin-flip call.
    18	
    19	## Base
    20	
    21	- Branch `research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop` @ 04026e94fb = PR #8753 head
    22	  (`mcwitt/8317-hero-step-optims`, based on xla-fork/#8684).
    23	- Contents: offload_carry remat + latency-hiding scheduler (forced
    24	  overlap-limit 1), bitcast pair-interleave, FA4 128x64 wide forward tile as
    25	  `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` (landed by implementation name; dispatch has passed unit
    26	  tests but has NOT yet run on a rack — the iteration-0 control validates it).
    27	- PJRT wheel: `jax_cuda13_pjrt-0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272` pinned in
    28	  pyproject/uv.lock (GitHub release, marin-xla-pjrt-20260828). No wheel
    29	  overlay in arms.
    30	- Reference numbers: 24k-restore same-night pair (2026-08-28): control (#8753
    31	  stack sans tile) 23.06/23.05, +tile 23.32/23.37 MFU. 30k-restore cross-ref:
    32	  plain-#8684 gate 22.31 median. Protocols are not comparable; this campaign
    33	  re-baselines at 30k.
    34	
    35	## Fixed protocol (every arm)
    36	
    37	- One NVL72 rack, cw-us-east-08a, `experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics`,
    38	  one arm on the rack at a time.
    39	- Restore from the latest hero permanent checkpoint
    40	  `s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000`
    41	  (verified complete: manifest.json present). `--num-steps 30030` (ABSOLUTE
    42	  stop step: checkpoint step + 30; `--num-steps 30` exits vacuously right
    43	  after restore).
    44	- Mixture data (trained-router routing), cf 1.15, `ragged_all_to_all`,
    45	  `--master-params device` (this branch renamed the master-less mode
    46	  `disabled` -> `device`; in-process migration reads a master-bearing
    47	  checkpoint's fp32 master directly into device params), schedule-steps
    48	  4470000, no checkpoints/evals. Profiler off on scored arms EXCEPT an
    49	  allowed profile tail: `PROFILE_STEPS` few, `PROFILE_START_STEP` ABSOLUTE
    50	  (e.g. 30021) so it lands past the scoring window — profiler start_step is
    51	  a global step; a relative value like 5 silently never fires on a restore.
    52	- `JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR` rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge)
    53	  → every arm cold-compiles ~9.5 min; pre-phase (env sync + restore +
    54	  migration) ~19 min cold, less when the object cache is warm.
    55	- Priority: production allowed (user 2026-08-28) with job runtime capped
    56	  under 1 hour: iris `--timeout 3500`. Watchdog: COMPILE_CEILING=1800 from
    57	  first W&B-run existence, STEP_BUDGET=1200 (20-min post-compile cap, user),
    58	  early release at +19 steps, cancel on preemption/failure/coord death;
    59	  QUEUE_CEILING bounds pending time (queueing doesn't count against the 1h).
    60	- SERIALIZATION: one arm on the rack at a time, and arm N+1 is submitted only
    61	  after arm N's watchdog exits — the coordinator's iris `--timeout 3500`
    62	  clock starts when the coordinator starts (it schedules instantly and waits
    63	  on its child), so an arm queued behind another arm burns its 1h budget in
    64	  the queue and gets killed just as its train job starts.
    65	- RESUBMISSION: an invalid arm (watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete
    66	  window) is resubmitted with a FRESH RID and VERSION, never reused — a
    67	  reused RID merges W&B histories (silent window mixing), reuses the
    68	  leader-populated compile cache (the documented clique-init deadlock
    69	  recipe), and can vacuously reuse the artifact layer.
    70	- Hero liveness (CORRECTED 2026-08-29): the hero IS live, training on its
    71	  own ~11 racks of cw-us-east-08a (177 pods, `iris job list` does not show
    72	  it — check pods/Kueue, not job-list greps). This is the normal operating
    73	  condition; the campaign uses the single leftover NVL72 rack and contends
    74	  only with other experiments (mokcamp, zack gangs) via same-band FIFO, no
    75	  preemption. Occupancy check before each arm: Kueue workloads + pods on
    76	  the 08a context, not `iris job list`.
    77	- TIMEOUT SIZING (lesson from the mfl-ctrl-a crash): iris `--timeout` on
    78	  the coordinator covers QUEUE WAIT plus run; a 3500s timeout dies in the
    79	  Kueue gate behind a one-hour occupant. Rack occupancy is intrinsically
    80	  bounded by NUM_STEPS=30030 (~30 steps past restore, <1h even with no
    81	  watchdog), so the <1h-runtime rule is enforced by the watchdog from
    82	  admission (compile ceiling starts at W&B-run existence) while ARM_TIMEOUT
    83	  is sized for the queue: default 28800 (8h) with watchdog
    84	  QUEUE_CEILING=25200 (7h) cancelling a still-gated gang first.
    85	- Rack etiquette: queue arms back-to-back while work is pending; do not
    86	  submit anything if the loop is blocked >1h on analysis.
    87	- Timestamp bookkeeping (calibration): for each arm record W&B-run-created
    88	  -> first-step wall time. The compile ceiling starts at run creation, which
    89	  is BEFORE restore + master migration + compile; a ceiling kill on a cold
    90	  arm is not evidence against the arm until these timings say the ceiling
    91	  actually fits the work.
    92	
    93	## Metric and guards
    94	
    95	- Metric (CALIBRATED at iteration 0, before any treatment comparison): the
    96	  primary decision statistic is the RUN MEDIAN of `throughput/mfu` over
    97	  scored steps +5..+19. Iteration-0 controls showed sparse environmental
    98	  stall steps (cross-node skew stragglers, 0/4/4 per draw, each -1.5 to -3
    99	  MFU for one step) that dominate run means (sd 0.255) while medians stay
   100	  tight (sd 0.053) — same phenomenon and remedy as the 260811 loop's
   101	  median switch. Stall count (steps >3% below the run median) is reported
   102	  per arm and guards the median's blind spot: a treatment whose draws
   103	  consistently raise stall counts is flagged regardless of median. Mean is
   104	  still recorded.
   105	- Guards (from the same run): `moe/drop_fraction` max/mean not worse than the
   106	  paired control; `train/loss` at the window's last step equivalent to
   107	  control (bf16-noise band; 24k arms showed 1.29964-65 across four arms);
   108	  `window_complete` must be true.
   109	- Noise model: only ONE NVL72 rack exists, so there is no placement variance
   110	  (user-supplied fact, 2026-08-29; overrides the ±2pp cross-night figure,
   111	  which came from multi-rack clusters) — residual noise is neighbor jobs,
   112	  clocks/thermals, and run-to-run scheduling jitter. The decision statistic
   113	  is the DIFFERENCE OF RUN-MEDIAN MFU, with run-level sd estimated from
   114	  the >=3 iteration-0 control draws (sd̂_run(median)=0.053; baseline
   115	  medians 23.3208/23.2400/23.3395) and refreshed by later controls;
   116	  per-step points within a run are autocorrelated and are never treated
   117	  as independent replicates. KEEP requires >=2 treatment draws and >=2
   118	  same-session control draws (bracketing), median delta > max(0.15 MFU,
   119	  3 x sd̂_run) = 0.16 at current calibration; a single C/T pair never
   120	  keeps. Calibrated guard bands (2x the worst observed across three C-C
   121	  pairs): drops max <= 1.7e-4; pointwise loss delta max <= 1.05e-4,
   122	  median <= 4e-5. Near-threshold deltas get
   123	  additional draws instead of a call. Periodic re-control draws guard
   124	  against slow drift across the campaign.
   125	- Fidelity gate for keeps: paired-window comparison of the FULL 15-step loss
   126	  series (data is deterministic at a fixed restore step so series pair
   127	  pointwise). The tolerance is CALIBRATED, not assumed: the iteration-0
   128	  control-vs-control pointwise deltas define the null band for this exact
   129	  30k protocol (the 24k figures are a different protocol). Gate on the
   130	  median pointwise delta plus the calibrated max (median-not-max
   131	  discriminates bf16 reduction noise from a real bug — prior campaign
   132	  lesson); drops get a tolerance from the same control pairs rather than a
   133	  raw "not worse" coin flip. A small exceedance triggers another paired
   134	  draw or a numerical investigation, NOT an automatic kill; non-finite
   135	  loss, materially changed drops, or a deviation beyond the control
   136	  envelope is a hard fail. Any keep that changes numerics beyond
   137	  scheduling/layout gets flagged and needs explicit reasoning about why
   138	  the trajectory is unchanged.
   139	
   140	## Iteration accounting
   141	
   142	Per user: only hypotheses that reach the rack count toward the 24-iteration
   143	floor; review-killed ideas do not. Campaign runs 24 rack iterations or 24 h,
   144	whichever comes LAST. Iteration 0 = 30k-protocol baseline (control draw(s)).
   145

   151	- H0 baseline+validation: >=3 control draws at 30k to establish the metric

   155	  first rack outing, and cross-night MFU deltas cannot confirm dispatch —
   156	  and (b) refreshes the lever-sourcing profile (H1 folded in: no separate
   157	  profile arm). Draw 2 runs SCORE_MAX_STEP=28: the default early release at
   158	  +19 would cancel at 30019, before the 30021 trace (codex finding). If the
   159	  tail perturbs draw 2's scored window relative to draws 1/3, drop draw 2
   160	  from the noise estimate.
   161	- H1 profile refresh: FOLDED INTO H0 draw 2 (see above); prior profile
   162	  (dk-hero, pre-offload) showed marshal 2.55 s as top lever and a dense-MLP
   163	  leg that turned out to be architecture (2 shared experts + remat) — both
   164	  priors need re-confirmation on the offload/LHS/tile stack.
   165	- H2 marshal-step levers (SO-H2): DEAD 2026-08-29 — the draw-b trace shows
   166	  zero marshal events and 20 ms/step pure starvation; the 2.55 s leg was
   167	  eliminated somewhere in the offload/LHS/tile stack. Do not re-run.
   168	- H3 transport-exposure hiding: the draw-b trace shows 1133 ms/step of
   169	  ragged-a2a EXPOSED (56% of its 2021 ms; 12 chunk ops x 48 launches).
   170	  Transport is SM-bound (prior campaign), so the lever is overlap/
   171	  pipelining of chunk ops with expert GEMMs, not a faster transport.
   172	  Needs a concrete treatment + engagement check before it can run.
   173	- H9 loop-carry D2D copies (NEW, from draw-b trace): 785 ms/step of
   174	  compute-stream MemcpyD2D — nine copy.7xx ops moving 1.85-3.22 GB, 48
   175	  launches each, per-layer while-loop buffer copies. Candidate: buffer
   176	  donation/aliasing in the layer loop, or moving them off the compute
   177	  stream. Code-level XLA/jax lever; needs investigation of which HLO
   178	  buffers these are before a treatment exists.
   179	- H10 offload-copy prefetch (NEW, from draw-b trace): 576 ms/step of
   180	  layer-carry D2H/H2D exposed while compute idles (~4-5 ms x ~90-140
   181	  copies/stream). Candidate: earlier prefetch / later writeback in the
   182	  offload schedule; interacts with H6 (slop-90 may change remat around the
   183	  offload names). Engagement check: exposed-memcpy time in a repeat
   184	  profile tail.
   185	- H4 capacity-factor titration at the restored router: hero drops at cf 1.15
   186	  are ~5e-5 — far under any budget; cf 1.10/1.05 shrinks transport+GEMM
   187	  buffers. GATE (review finding): a cf keep changes training dynamics and
   188	  its drop cost varies with data over the whole run — a 15-step window
   189	  cannot bound it. Pre-declared acceptance: drop_fraction_max <= 1e-3 in
   190	  the window AND loss series within the control band; even then a cf keep
   191	  is flagged for explicit user sign-off before hero promotion, and the
   192	  prior loop note (2026-08-12: CF moves are frontier-walking) is quoted in
   193	  the decision. NOTE (codex): the 15-step window cannot establish whole-run
   194	  loss equivalence for a dynamics-touching change; H4's in-window
   195	  measurement establishes the THROUGHPUT effect only, and any promotion
   196	  decision belongs to the user.
   197	- H5 overlap-limit re-titration: DEAD AS A FLAG ARM on this base —
   198	  `offload_carry` force-sets overlap-limit 1 non-overridably and
   199	  `_apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults` silently strips conflicting
   200	  ARM_XLA_FLAGS (a flag-based treatment would run as an exact control).
   201	  Testing overlap>1 means changing the remat mode, which is a different
   202	  hypothesis (and the documented silent-corruption recipe with LHS +
   203	  offload — see activation-offload-lhs-overlap-race).
   204	- H6 slop-factor raise (user suggestion 2026-08-29, HIGH priority — cheap,
   205	  code-supported mechanism): `--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85` in
   206	  train.py flag_defaults was calibrated for the pre-offload posture (default
   207	  95 sized the temp arena at 125.7 GiB > the 120.2 GiB the pool can serve;
   208	  85 -> 113.6 GiB), and the comment itself says a smaller arena makes
   209	  HloRematerialization recompute more of the step. The carry offload since
   210	  dropped peak from ~138 to ~116.5 GiB, so the remat pressure the arena cap
   211	  creates may now be pure waste. The flag IS overridable via inherited
   212	  XLA_FLAGS (verified: only overlap-limit and ragged-required flags are
   213	  forced), so treatment = ARM_XLA_FLAGS with slop 90 (arena ~120.2, at the
   214	  documented bound; ladder to 88 on OOM, 92/95 on green+gain). Fidelity:
   215	  remat changes recompute scheduling only. Engagement check: peak-HBM
   216	  metric must RISE vs control (less remat holds more live); an unchanged
   217	  peak means the flag did not engage regardless of the MFU reading. Failure
   218	  mode is a named first-step NCCL allocation abort — cheap crash, ladder
   219	  down.
   220	- H7 attn-offload re-add: 23.14 but 3x stdev and the host allocation lands as
   221	  one contiguous 72.56 GiB block regardless of HOST_MEM_LIMIT_GB — ledger
   222	  says dead unless pod memory grows.
   223	- H8 marin_ep_mgpu_fused transport on this stack: mok-goal showed 22.83 vs
   224	  22.30 control at 18k restore with 16x fewer drops, but needs the kmax128
   225	  wheel (conflicts with the pinned c9526e wheel) and the backend isn't on
   226	  this branch. Big integration; park unless transport dominates H1 profile
   227	  and cheaper levers dry up.
   228	- Dead-lever ledger (do not re-run without new evidence): PGLE (null),
   229	  command buffers at hero shape (+7.5 GiB, negative), FA4 backward tile
   230	  (hard-locked 64x64), 128x128 forward tile (2.5x regression), fused shared
   231	  experts (~0), small-param FSDP (negative), unchaining per-chunk a2a
   232	  buffers (-0.13), NVLS/SHARP env (null), fa4-lse primal output (moot: XLA

 succeeded in 557ms:
[pr:18582] [grug] Offload the layer carry and schedule ragged EP for latency
pr · mcwitt · 2026-08-28T17:23:37Z
https://github.com/marin-community/marin/pull/8753

Carry offload — hypothesis: the latency-hiding scheduler would help the ragged transport, but its longer buffer live ranges overflow HBM (NCCL's first-step allocations fail). Implementation: a new `offload_carry` remat mode stages each block's residual-stream input on pinned host via `tree_checkpoint_name`; the scheduler turns on only under this mode, and only launchers measured with the offload take it (one `with_transport_remat_mode` helper). Effect: +0.44 MFU and a 21 GiB lower HBM peak. Caveat: offload + scheduler + collective overlap limit above 1 silently corrupts training (the reloaded residual races its consumer in backward; first-step loss lands near 9.5) while no pair of the three does, so the offload forces the limit to 1 — a default would be droppable by an inherited `XLA_FLAGS` entry.

[issue:55141] comment on #8317 — [grug] Ragged EP64 hero: match fixed-transport throughput below 2% drops
issue · mcwitt · 2026-08-22T13:49:25Z
https://github.com/marin-community/marin/issues/8317#issuecomment-5380743393

🤖 Leg-2 result: the ragged transport now beats pooled-wave on throughput, drops, and loss.

The structural rewrite (branch `research/mcwitt/8317-ragged-tune`) replaces the ragged backend's repeat/sort/compact/local-permute machinery with expert-granular `ragged_all_to_all` updates: senders read accepted prefixes at unclipped offsets, receiver offsets pack arrivals expert-major, and the dispatch buffer is built with one gather. The lean graph could not run unchunked — XLA never rematerializes collectives, so the exposed a2a outputs pinned 192–194 GiB against 184 GiB HBM across four launch postures — and landing it required processing local experts in two sequential chunks with per-chunk capacity, plus a splits-per-peer retune to 64.

## TL;DR

Campaign start. The ragged all-to-all hero (E384, top-8, i3072, EP64 on one NVL72 rack) runs at 20.20% MFU / 223k tokens/s (`ra2a-mw-g17`, cf 1.00, LR≈0 harness). The goal is throughput comparable to the fixed-transport `moe_hero_ep` template (~25% MFU, ~300k tokens/s per rack) while holding steady-state trained-router drops under 2%.

    85	  }
    86	  const int64_t min_bytes = debug_options.xla_gpu_async_copy_min_bytes();
    87	  if (min_bytes == -1) {
    88	    return false;
    89	  }
    90	
    91	  bool changed = false;
    92	  for (HloComputation* computation :
    93	       module->MakeNonfusionComputations(execution_threads)) {
    94	    if (computation->IsAsyncComputation()) {
    95	      continue;
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    std::vector<HloInstruction*> instructions =
    99	        computation->MakeInstructionPostOrder();
   100	    for (HloInstruction* instr : instructions) {
   101	      if (!IsEligibleD2DCopy(instr, min_bytes)) {
   102	        continue;
   103	      }
   104	
   105	      ABSL_RETURN_IF_ERROR(computation
   106	                          ->CreateAsyncInstructions(
   107	                              instr, {ShapeUtil::MakeScalarShape(U32)})
   108	                          .status());
   109	      changed = true;
   110	    }
   111	  }
   112	
   113	  return changed;
   114	}
   115	
   116	}  // namespace xla::gpu

    20	#include "absl/status/statusor.h"
    21	#include "absl/strings/string_view.h"
    22	#include "xla/hlo/ir/hlo_module.h"
    23	#include "xla/hlo/pass/hlo_pass_interface.h"
    24	
    25	namespace xla::gpu {
    26	
    27	// Converts eligible device-to-device HLO `copy` instructions into
    28	// `copy-start`/`copy-done` pairs so that the latency-hiding scheduler can
    29	// overlap the D2D memcpy with compute-bound kernels on the main stream.
    30	//
    31	// The pass is disabled when `xla_gpu_async_copy_min_bytes` is -1. Otherwise, a
    32	// copy is eligible when:
    33	//   - Both source and destination reside in device memory (no host memory
    34	//     space annotation), i.e. the copy is a true D2D transfer.
    35	//   - Source and destination layouts are identical, so the copy is a plain
    36	//     memcpy rather than a layout-changing transpose.
    37	//   - The transfer size is at or above `xla_gpu_async_copy_min_bytes` to
    38	//     amortize stream-synchronization overhead.
    39	//   - The instruction is not already inside an async computation.
    40	//
    41	// The resulting `copy-start`/`copy-done` pair is handled by existing machinery:
    42	//   - `ExecutionStreamAssignment` assigns a `ComputationStreamId`.
    43	//   - `ThunkEmitter::EmitCopyStartThunk` wraps the copy in an `AsyncStartThunk`
    44	//     that issues the memcpy on an auxiliary compute stream.
    45	//   - `ThunkEmitter::EmitCopyDoneThunk` emits an `AsyncDoneThunk` that makes
    46	//     the main stream wait for completion.
    47	//   - The LHS scheduler maximises the overlap window between start and done.
    48	class GpuCopyAsyncWrapper : public HloModulePass {
    49	 public:
    50	  absl::string_view name() const override { return "gpu-copy-async-wrapper"; }
    51	
    52	 protected:
    53	  absl::StatusOr<bool> RunImpl(
    54	      HloModule* module,
    55	      const absl::flat_hash_set<absl::string_view>& execution_threads) override;
    56	};
    57	
    58	}  // namespace xla::gpu
    59	
    60	#endif  // XLA_BACKENDS_GPU_TRANSFORMS_GPU_COPY_ASYNC_WRAPPER_H_

   211	  for (const HloInstruction* instr :
   212	       module->entry_computation()->instructions()) {
   213	    if (instr->opcode() == HloOpcode::kCopyStart) {
   214	      copy_start = instr;
   215	    }
   216	  }
   217	  ASSERT_NE(copy_start, nullptr);
   218	  ASSERT_EQ(copy_start->control_predecessors().size(), 1);
   219	  EXPECT_EQ(copy_start->control_predecessors()[0]->name(), "add");

   689	// Returns how many instructions using the given resource_type we can overlap
   690	int64_t GpuAsyncTracker::GetNumAvailableResources(int64_t resource_type) const {
   691	  CHECK_LT(resource_type,
   692	           ResourceTypeToIndex(GpuResourceType::kGpuResourceTypeEnd));

   694	    return GpuAsyncTrackerBase::GetNumAvailableResources(resource_type);

   697	  // We will allow upto 1 outstanding collective on the async stream. This
   698	  // controls the number of collectives in flight in the schedule (a
   699	  // collective is in flight if the start is issued but not done). As an
   700	  // example, with 1, LHS will generate the schedule: s0,e0,s1,e1, i.e., s1
   701	  // is not scheduled until e0 is scheduled. With 2, the scheduler can
   702	  // schedule s0,s1,e0,e1, because it assumes that the 2 instances of the
   703	  // resources do not interfere with each other. If we do want to support > 1
   704	  // async stream, we can increase this number and then do a post-pass on the
   705	  // scheduled code to assign async stream-id to collectives (and actually
   706	  // support > 1 async stream in the runtime).
   707	  // The only case we'd allow 2 for now is when the current resource is
   708	  // for an async computation operation which will be allocated with
   709	  // a dedicated compute stream. It can run concurrently with
   710	  // another collective.
   711	  if (resource_type ==
   712	      ResourceTypeToIndex(GpuResourceType::kGpuAsyncStreamComputes)) {
   713	    return config_.parallel_async_compute_limit;
   714	  }

    39	
    40	// Returns true if `shape` carries a host-memory-space annotation.
    41	bool HasHostMemorySpace(const Shape& shape) {
    42	  if (!shape.has_layout()) {
    43	    return false;
    44	  }
    45	  return shape.layout().memory_space() ==
    46	         static_cast<int64_t>(stream_executor::MemorySpace::kHost);
    47	}
    48	
    49	// Returns true if `instr` is eligible for async D2D wrapping.
    50	bool IsEligibleD2DCopy(const HloInstruction* instr, int64_t min_copy_bytes) {
    51	  if (instr->opcode() != HloOpcode::kCopy) {
    52	    return false;
    53	  }
    54	  const Shape& shape = instr->shape();
    55	  // Only wrap array shapes; tuples are not directly copyable via D2D memcpy.
    56	  if (!shape.IsArray()) {
    57	    return false;
    58	  }
    59	  // Skip copies involving host memory — those are handled by other paths.
    60	  if (HasHostMemorySpace(shape) ||
    61	      HasHostMemorySpace(instr->operand(0)->shape())) {
    62	    return false;
    63	  }
    64	  // Only wrap copies that are plain memcpys: a layout-changing copy is a
    65	  // transpose in disguise and must stay on the kernel emission path.
    66	  if (!LayoutUtil::LayoutsInShapesEqual(shape, instr->operand(0)->shape(),
    67	                                        Layout::Equal().MinorToMajorOnly())) {
    68	    return false;
    69	  }
    70	  // Enforce the minimum size threshold to amortize sync overhead.
    71	  if (ShapeUtil::ByteSizeOf(shape) < min_copy_bytes) {
    72	    return false;
    73	  }
    74	  return true;
    75	}

   606	//===----------------------------------------------------------------------===//
   607	// GpuAsyncTracker
   608	//===----------------------------------------------------------------------===//
   609	GpuAsyncTracker::GpuAsyncTracker(const SchedulerConfig& config)
   610	    : GpuAsyncTrackerBase(config) {}
   611	
   612	static bool IsAnnotatedForGpuAsyncStreamCollectivesP2P(
   613	    const HloInstruction& instr) {
   614	  const HloInstruction& start_instr =
   615	      GpuGetCanonicalAsyncOp(instr).outer == HloOpcode::kAsyncDone
   616	          ? *instr.operand(0)
   617	          : instr;
   618	  auto it =
   619	      start_instr.frontend_attributes().map().find(kCollectiveStreamAttrName);
   620	  if (it == start_instr.frontend_attributes().map().end()) return false;
   621	  return it->second == kCollectiveStreamP2P;
   622	}
   623	
   624	ResourcesVector GpuAsyncTracker::GetResourcesFromInstructionImpl(
   625	    const HloInstruction& instr) const {
   626	  CanonicalAsyncOp op = GetCanonicalAsyncOp(instr);
   627	  if (op.outer == HloOpcode::kAsyncStart || op.outer == HloOpcode::kAsyncDone) {
   628	    ResourceUsageType usage;
   629	    GpuResourceType resource;
   630	
   631	    // Keep existing copy-start/copy-done and host-memory slicing fusions on the
   632	    // async-compute resource path. Only dynamic-slice memcpy async wrappers use
   633	    // the dedicated memcpy resource added for D2D copy overlap.
   634	    if (IsDynamicSliceCopyFusionAsyncOp(instr)) {
   635	      resource = GpuResourceType::kGpuAsyncStreamMemcpy;
   636	      usage = op.outer == HloOpcode::kAsyncStart
   637	                  ? (config_.top_down_scheduling
   638	                         ? ResourceUsageType::kResourceOccupy
   639	                         : ResourceUsageType::kResourceRelease)
   640	                  : (config_.top_down_scheduling
   641	                         ? ResourceUsageType::kResourceRelease
   642	                         : ResourceUsageType::kResourceOccupy);
   643	    } else if (IsAnnotatedForGpuAsyncStreamCollectivesP2P(instr)) {
   644	      resource = GpuResourceType::kGpuAsyncStreamCollectivesP2P;
   645	      usage = op.outer == HloOpcode::kAsyncStart
   646	                  ? ResourceUsageType::kResourceRelease
   647	                  : ResourceUsageType::kResourceOccupy;
   648	    } else if (op.inner == HloOpcode::kSend || op.inner == HloOpcode::kRecv) {
   649	      std::tie(resource, usage) = GetP2PResourceAndUsage(instr, op);
   650	    } else {
   651	      usage = op.outer == HloOpcode::kAsyncStart
   652	                  ? ResourceUsageType::kResourceRelease
   653	                  : ResourceUsageType::kResourceOccupy;
   654	      resource = hlo_query::IsCollectiveCommunicationOp(op.inner) ||
   655	                         IsCustomCollectiveOp(&instr) ||
   656	                         IsCollectivesGroupAsyncOp(instr)
   657	                     ? GpuResourceType::kGpuAsyncStreamCollectives
   658	                     : GpuResourceType::kGpuAsyncStreamComputes;
   659	    }
   660	    return {std::make_pair(ResourceTypeToIndex(resource), usage)};
   661	  }
   662	  return GpuAsyncTrackerBase::GetResourcesFromInstructionImpl(instr);
   663	}
   664	
   665	absl::flat_hash_map<int64_t, int64_t>
   666	GpuAsyncTracker::GetNumResourcesPerInstruction(
   667	    const HloInstruction& instr) const {
   668	  if (!IsCollectivesGroupAsyncOp(instr)) {
   669	    return GpuAsyncTrackerBase::GetNumResourcesPerInstruction(instr);
   670	  }

   673	  // count the collectives in its wrapped computation as separate resources.
   674	  absl::flat_hash_map<int64_t, int64_t> resource_counts;
   675	  for (const auto& [resource_type, usage] :

  1029	      module.result_shape(),
  1030	      [&](const Shape& subshape, const ShapeIndex& /*index*/) {
  1031	        total_io_size += get_device_shape_size(subshape);
  1032	      });
  1033	
  1034	  // If any inputs and outputs are aliased, do not double count them.
  1035	  module.input_output_alias_config().ForEachAlias(
  1036	      [&](const ShapeIndex& output_index,
  1037	          const HloInputOutputAliasConfig::Alias&) {
  1038	        const Shape& subshape =
  1039	            ShapeUtil::GetSubshape(module.result_shape(), output_index);
  1040	        total_io_size -= get_device_shape_size(subshape);
  1041	      });
  1042	
  1043	  if (total_io_size > base_limit) {
  1044	    LOG(ERROR) << "The byte size of input/output arguments (" << total_io_size
  1045	               << ") exceeds the base limit (" << base_limit
  1046	               << "). This indicates an error in the calculation!";
  1047	    return 0;
  1048	  }
  1049	
  1050	  return (base_limit - total_io_size) *
  1051	         module.config().debug_options().xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor() /
  1052	         100;
  1053	}
  1054	
  1055	SchedulerConfig MakeGPUSchedulerConfig(uint64_t memory_limit,
  1056	                                       int64_t overlap_limit,
  1057	                                       int64_t async_compute_limit) {
  1058	  SchedulerConfig config;
  1059	  config.all_reduce_overlap_limit = 1;
  1060	  config.collective_broadcast_overlap_limit = 1;
  1061	  config.collective_permute_overlap_limit = 1;
  1062	  config.use_real_cost_model = false;
  1063	  config.aggressive_scheduling_policies = true;
  1064	  config.schedule_send_recvs = true;
  1065	  config.memory_limit = memory_limit;
  1066	  config.parallel_collective_overlap_limit = overlap_limit;
  1067	  config.parallel_async_compute_limit = async_compute_limit;
  1068	
  1069	  CHECK(config.collective_broadcast_overlap_limit <=
  1070	        config.parallel_collective_overlap_limit);
  1071	  CHECK(config.all_to_all_overlap_limit <=
  1072	        config.parallel_collective_overlap_limit);
  1073	  CHECK(config.all_gather_overlap_limit <=
  1074	        config.parallel_collective_overlap_limit);
  1075	  CHECK(config.all_reduce_overlap_limit <=
  1076	        config.parallel_collective_overlap_limit);
  1077	  CHECK(config.reduce_scatter_overlap_limit <=
  1078	        config.parallel_collective_overlap_limit);
  1079	
  1080	  return config;

    18	
    19	#include "xla/backends/gpu/transforms/double_buffer_loop_unrolling.h"

    24	
    25	namespace xla {
    26	namespace gpu {
    27	
    28	// Returns true if the pass is enabled via `optimization_level` at
    29	// the potential expense of compile time.
    30	template <typename Pass>
    31	bool IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort(const HloModule& module) {
    32	  bool is_collective_optimization_pass =
    33	      std::is_same_v<Pass, CollectivePipeliner> ||
    34	      std::is_same_v<Pass, DoubleBufferLoopUnrolling> ||
    35	      std::is_same_v<Pass, LatencyHidingScheduler>;
    36	
    37	  if (is_collective_optimization_pass) {
    38	    ExecutionOptions::EffortLevel opt_level =
    39	        module.config().optimization_level();
    40	
    41	    return opt_level >= ExecutionOptions::EFFORT_O1;
    42	  }
    43	
    44	  return true;
    45	}
    46	
    47	}  // namespace gpu
    48	}  // namespace xla
    49	
    50	#endif  // XLA_SERVICE_GPU_FLAG_UTILS_H_

    30	
    31	TEST(FlagUtilsTest, IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationLevel) {
    32	  HloModule module("test_module", {});
    33	
    34	  for (ExecutionOptions::EffortLevel level :
    35	       {ExecutionOptions::EFFORT_O1, ExecutionOptions::EFFORT_O2,
    36	        ExecutionOptions::EFFORT_O3}) {
    37	    HloModuleConfig config;
    38	    config.set_optimization_level(level);
    39	    module.set_config(config);
    40	
    41	    // Collective optimization passes.
    42	    EXPECT_TRUE(IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort<CollectivePipeliner>(module));
    43	    EXPECT_TRUE(
    44	        IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort<DoubleBufferLoopUnrolling>(module));
    45	    EXPECT_TRUE(
    46	        IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort<LatencyHidingScheduler>(module));
    47	
    48	    // Other passes.
    49	    EXPECT_TRUE(IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort<HloDCE>(module));
    50	  }
    51	
    52	  HloModuleConfig config;
    53	  config.set_optimization_level(ExecutionOptions::EFFORT_O0);
    54	  module.set_config(config);
    55	
    56	  // Collective optimization passes.
    57	  EXPECT_FALSE(IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort<CollectivePipeliner>(module));
    58	  EXPECT_FALSE(
    59	      IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort<DoubleBufferLoopUnrolling>(module));
    60	  EXPECT_FALSE(
    61	      IsPassEnabledAtOptimizationEffort<LatencyHidingScheduler>(module));
    62	}
    63	
    64	}  // namespace
    65	}  // namespace gpu
    66	}  // namespace xla

class CompilerEffortLevel(enum.Enum):
  """Effort level enumeration for XLA.

  Used to specify the degree to which the XLA compiler should optimize for
  runtime performance or memory fitting, as described in
  https://openxla.org/xla/effort_levels. Higher effort levels will expend
  more compile time but should yield better results in the respective
  dimension.
  """

  UNKNOWN = 0
  O0 = 9
  O1 = 19
  O2 = 29
  O3 = 39

  @classmethod
  def _missing_(cls, value: object) -> CompilerEffortLevel | None:
    return _effort_from_string.get(value)


_effort_from_string: dict[Any, CompilerEffortLevel] = {
    "UNKNOWN": CompilerEffortLevel.UNKNOWN,
    "O0": CompilerEffortLevel.O0,
    "O1": CompilerEffortLevel.O1,
    "O2": CompilerEffortLevel.O2,
    "O3": CompilerEffortLevel.O3,
}
_DISABLE_MOST_OPTIMIZATIONS = config.bool_flag(
    'jax_disable_most_optimizations',
    config.bool_env('JAX_DISABLE_MOST_OPTIMIZATIONS', False),
    'Try not to do much optimization work. This can be useful if the cost of '
    'optimization is greater than that of running a less-optimized program.')

_COMPILER_DETAILED_LOGGING_MIN_OPS = config.int_flag(
    "jax_compiler_detailed_logging_min_ops",
    config.int_env("JAX_COMPILER_DETAILED_LOGGING_MIN_OPS", 10),
    help=(
        'How big should a module be in MLIR operations before JAX enables '
        'detailed compiler logging? The intent of this flag is to suppress '
        'detailed logging for small/uninteresting computations.'
    ),
)

# The special XLA-AutoFDO profile version that indicates that a profile is not
# available and retrieval should not be attempted.
_NO_PROFILE_DONT_RETRIEVE = -1

traceback_util.register_exclusion(__file__)

CompileOptions = xc.CompileOptions

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def _add_disabled_hlo_pass(disabled_passes: str, pass_name: str) -> str:
  """Adds a pass to a comma-separated pass list, preserving existing entries.

  ``DebugOptions.xla_disable_hlo_passes`` may already hold passes the user
  disabled via ``XLA_FLAGS=--xla_disable_hlo_passes=...``; those must not be
  overwritten. Entries are stripped because XLA matches pass names exactly.
  """
  passes = [p.strip() for p in disabled_passes.split(",") if p.strip()]
  if pass_name not in passes:
    passes.append(pass_name)
  return ",".join(passes)


def _get_cross_compile_backend(compile_only_backend):
  """Returns a real backend for cross-compilation via a real client.

  When cross-compiling via a compile-only client, checks if a real backend
  is available for the same platform. If so, returns it so compilation can
  leverage real hardware (e.g., for GPU kernel autotuning).
  """
  platform = compile_only_backend.platform
  try:
    real_backend = xb.get_backend(platform)
  except Exception:
    return None
  # Don't use the real backend if it's also a compile-only client.
  if isinstance(real_backend, _jax.CompileOnlyPyClient):
    return None
  # Don't use real backend if platform version is different, this can lead
  # to timeouts and hangs.
  if real_backend.platform_version != compile_only_backend.platform_version:
    return None
  return real_backend


# Will be monkeypatched with the function that gets the XLA-AutoFDO profile
# version. The default (-1) takes care of errors.
# TODO(b/289098047): consider refactoring this interface.
def get_latest_profile_version(backend: xc.Client) -> int:
  del backend
  return -1


def _walk_operations(op, k):
  k -= 1
  if k < 0:
    return k
  for region in op.regions:
    for block in region:
      for child_op in block:
        k = _walk_operations(child_op, k)
        if k < 0:
          return k
  return k


def use_detailed_logging(module: ir.Module) -> bool:
  """Returns 'true' if detailed logging should be enabled for 'module'."""
  bound = _COMPILER_DETAILED_LOGGING_MIN_OPS.value
  return _walk_operations(module.operation, bound) < 0


def log_persistent_cache_hit(module_name: str, cache_key: str) -> None:
  hit_log_priority = (logging.WARNING if config.log_compiles.value
                      else logging.DEBUG)
  logger.log(hit_log_priority, "Persistent compilation cache hit for '%s' with key %r",
             module_name, cache_key)


def log_persistent_cache_miss(module_name: str, cache_key: str) -> None:
  miss_log_priority = (logging.WARNING
                        if config.explain_cache_misses.value
                        and compilation_cache.is_persistent_cache_enabled()
                        else logging.DEBUG)
  # all caps to match the tracing cache "TRACING CACHE MISS"
  logger.log(miss_log_priority, "PERSISTENT COMPILATION CACHE MISS for '%s' with key %r",
             module_name, cache_key)


def get_compile_options(
    num_replicas: int,
    num_partitions: int,
    device_assignment=None,
    env_options_overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    fdo_profile: bytes | None = None,
    detailed_logging: bool = True,
    backend: xc.Client | None = None,
) -> xc.CompileOptions:
  """Returns the compile options to use, as derived from flag values.

  Args:
    num_replicas: Number of replicas for which to compile.
    num_partitions: Number of partitions for which to compile.
    device_assignment: Optional ndarray of jax devices indicating the assignment
      of logical replicas to physical devices (default inherited from
      xla_client.CompileOptions). Must be consistent with `num_replicas` and
      `num_partitions`.
    env_options_overrides: dict of additional options parsed by the compiler
    fdo_profile: Optional profile for feedback-directed optimization passed to
      XLA.
    detailed_logging: Is this an "interesting" computation about which XLA would
      be wise to log compilation information?
    backend: the client, if available.
  """
  compile_options = xc.CompileOptions()
  compile_options.num_replicas = num_replicas
  compile_options.num_partitions = num_partitions
  build_options = compile_options.executable_build_options
  build_options.use_spmd_partitioning = True
  build_options.use_shardy_partitioner = config.use_shardy_partitioner.value
  if fdo_profile is not None:
    build_options.fdo_profile = fdo_profile
  if device_assignment is not None:
    logger.debug(
        'get_compile_options: num_replicas=%s num_partitions=%s device_assignment=%s',
        num_replicas, num_partitions, device_assignment)
    device_assignment = np.array(device_assignment)

    # Allow 1D device assignment if num_partitions is 1.
    if (device_assignment.ndim == 1) and (num_partitions == 1):
      device_assignment = device_assignment[:, None]

    if num_replicas != device_assignment.shape[0]:
      msg = 'device_assignment does not match num_replicas: {} vs {}.'
      raise ValueError(msg.format(device_assignment, num_replicas))

    if num_partitions != device_assignment.shape[1]:
      msg = 'device_assignment does not match num_partitions: {} vs {}.'
      raise ValueError(msg.format(device_assignment, num_partitions))

    if device_assignment.dtype == object:
      device_assignment = np.vectorize(lambda d: d.id, otypes=[int])(
          device_assignment)
    device_assignment = xc.DeviceAssignment.create(device_assignment)
    assert device_assignment.replica_count() == num_replicas
    assert device_assignment.computation_count() == num_partitions
    compile_options.device_assignment = device_assignment

  build_options.optimization_level = CompilerEffortLevel(
      config.optimization_level.value
  ).value
  build_options.memory_fitting_level = CompilerEffortLevel(
      config.memory_fitting_level.value
  ).value

  if env_options_overrides is not None:
    # Some overrides are passed directly on build_options.
    overrides_on_build_options = ["optimization_level", "memory_fitting_level"]

    env_options_overrides = dict(env_options_overrides)
    for name in overrides_on_build_options:
      if name in env_options_overrides:
        setattr(
            build_options,
            name,
            CompilerEffortLevel(env_options_overrides.pop(name)).value,
        )
    compile_options.env_option_overrides = list(env_options_overrides.items())

  debug_options = compile_options.executable_build_options.debug_options

  2253	    enum_values=['off', 'on', 'auto'],
  2254	    default='auto',
  2255	    help=(
  2256	        'Enable experimental support for MAGMA-backed lax.linalg.eig on GPU. '
  2257	        'See the documentation for lax.linalg.eig for more details about how '
  2258	        'to use this feature.'
  2259	    ),
  2260	)
  2261	
  2262	optimization_level = enum_state(
  2263	    name='jax_optimization_level',
  2264	    enum_values=[
  2265	        'UNKNOWN',
  2266	        'O0',
  2267	        'O1',
  2268	        'O2',
  2269	        'O3',
  2270	    ],
  2271	    default='UNKNOWN',
  2272	    help='The degree to which the compiler should optimize for execution time',
  2273	    include_in_jit_key=True
  2274	)
  2275	
  2276	memory_fitting_level = enum_state(
  2277	    name='jax_memory_fitting_level',
  2278	    enum_values=[
  2279	        'UNKNOWN',
  2280	        'O0',
  2281	        'O1',
  2282	        'O2',
  2283	        'O3',
  2284	    ],
  2285	    default='O2',
  2286	    help=(
  2287	        'The degree to which the compiler should attempt to make the program'
  2288	        ' fit in memory'
  2289	    ),
  2290	    include_in_jit_key=True,
   180	def get_compile_options(
   181	    num_replicas: int,
   182	    num_partitions: int,
   183	    device_assignment=None,
   184	    env_options_overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None,
   185	    fdo_profile: bytes | None = None,
   186	    detailed_logging: bool = True,
   187	    backend: xc.Client | None = None,
   188	) -> xc.CompileOptions:
   189	  """Returns the compile options to use, as derived from flag values.
   190	
   191	  Args:
   192	    num_replicas: Number of replicas for which to compile.
   193	    num_partitions: Number of partitions for which to compile.
   194	    device_assignment: Optional ndarray of jax devices indicating the assignment
   195	      of logical replicas to physical devices (default inherited from
   196	      xla_client.CompileOptions). Must be consistent with `num_replicas` and
   197	      `num_partitions`.
   198	    env_options_overrides: dict of additional options parsed by the compiler
   199	    fdo_profile: Optional profile for feedback-directed optimization passed to
   200	      XLA.
   201	    detailed_logging: Is this an "interesting" computation about which XLA would
   202	      be wise to log compilation information?
   203	    backend: the client, if available.
   204	  """
   205	  compile_options = xc.CompileOptions()
   206	  compile_options.num_replicas = num_replicas
   207	  compile_options.num_partitions = num_partitions
   208	  build_options = compile_options.executable_build_options
   209	  build_options.use_spmd_partitioning = True
   210	  build_options.use_shardy_partitioner = config.use_shardy_partitioner.value
   211	  if fdo_profile is not None:
   212	    build_options.fdo_profile = fdo_profile
   213	  if device_assignment is not None:
   214	    logger.debug(
   215	        'get_compile_options: num_replicas=%s num_partitions=%s device_assignment=%s',
   216	        num_replicas, num_partitions, device_assignment)
   217	    device_assignment = np.array(device_assignment)
   218	
   219	    # Allow 1D device assignment if num_partitions is 1.
   220	    if (device_assignment.ndim == 1) and (num_partitions == 1):
   221	      device_assignment = device_assignment[:, None]
   222	
   223	    if num_replicas != device_assignment.shape[0]:
   224	      msg = 'device_assignment does not match num_replicas: {} vs {}.'
   225	      raise ValueError(msg.format(device_assignment, num_replicas))
   226	
   227	    if num_partitions != device_assignment.shape[1]:
   228	      msg = 'device_assignment does not match num_partitions: {} vs {}.'
   229	      raise ValueError(msg.format(device_assignment, num_partitions))
   230	
   231	    if device_assignment.dtype == object:
   232	      device_assignment = np.vectorize(lambda d: d.id, otypes=[int])(
   233	          device_assignment)
   234	    device_assignment = xc.DeviceAssignment.create(device_assignment)
   235	    assert device_assignment.replica_count() == num_replicas
   236	    assert device_assignment.computation_count() == num_partitions
   237	    compile_options.device_assignment = device_assignment
   238	
   239	  build_options.optimization_level = CompilerEffortLevel(
   240	      config.optimization_level.value
   241	  ).value
   242	  build_options.memory_fitting_level = CompilerEffortLevel(
   243	      config.memory_fitting_level.value
   244	  ).value
   245	
   246	  if env_options_overrides is not None:
   247	    # Some overrides are passed directly on build_options.
   248	    overrides_on_build_options = ["optimization_level", "memory_fitting_level"]
   249	
   250	    env_options_overrides = dict(env_options_overrides)
   251	    for name in overrides_on_build_options:
   252	      if name in env_options_overrides:
   253	        setattr(
   254	            build_options,
   255	            name,
