   297	
   298	absl::StatusOr<bool> HloPassPipeline::RunImpl(
   299	    HloModule* module,
   300	    const absl::flat_hash_set<absl::string_view>& execution_threads) {
   301	  run_called_ = true;
   302	
   303	  VLOG(1) << "Running HLO pass pipeline on module " << module->name() << ": "
   304	          << name();
   305	
   306	  tsl::profiler::TraceMe traceme([&] {
   307	    return tsl::profiler::TraceMeEncode(
   308	        absl::StrCat(name(), " (", module->name(), ")"),
   309	        {{"module", module->name()}});
   310	  });
   311	  // Copy debug options by value as passes may modify module config.
   312	  DebugOptions debug_options = module->config().debug_options();
   313	  return RunPassesInternal(module, debug_options, execution_threads);

  3214	HloCostAnalysis::Options CreateHloAnalysisOpts(
  3215	    const HloModule& module, const se::DeviceDescription& gpu_device_info,
  3216	    ShapeSizeFn shape_size_fn) {
  3217	  tsl::profiler::TraceMe traceme("CreateHloAnalysisOpts");
  3218	  HloCostAnalysis::Options hlo_cost_analysis_options;
  3219	  hlo_cost_analysis_options.shape_size = shape_size_fn;
  3220	  std::optional<HloRematerialization::HostMemoryOffloadConfig>
  3221	      offloading_config = std::nullopt;
  3222	  if (module.config().debug_options().xla_gpu_enable_host_memory_offloading()) {
  3223	    constexpr float kGiga = 1e+9;
  3224	    // Fused multiply-add means that these two instructions are computed as
  3225	    // one, so for this case the maximum flops is doubled.
  3226	    constexpr float kFma = 2;
  3227	    float flops_per_sec = gpu_device_info.core_count() *
  3228	                          gpu_device_info.fpus_per_core() *
  3229	                          gpu_device_info.clock_rate_ghz() * kGiga * kFma;
  3230	    int64_t host_memory_space_color =
  3231	        static_cast<int64_t>(stream_executor::MemorySpace::kHost);
  3232	    hlo_cost_analysis_options.set_flops_per_second(flops_per_sec);
  3233	    hlo_cost_analysis_options.set_transcendentals_per_second(flops_per_sec);
  3234	    offloading_config =
  3235	        std::make_optional<HloRematerialization::HostMemoryOffloadConfig>(
  3236	            /*host_memory_space=*/host_memory_space_color,
  3237	            /*bandwidth_to_host_bytes_per_second=*/
  3238	            gpu_device_info.memory_bandwidth(),
  3239	            /*bandwidth_from_host_bytes_per_second=*/
  3240	            gpu_device_info.memory_bandwidth());
  3241	  }
  3242	  return hlo_cost_analysis_options;
  3243	}
  3244	
  3245	HloRematerialization::Options CreateRematOpts(
  3246	    const HloModule& module, const se::DeviceDescription& gpu_device_info,
  3247	    HloCostAnalysis& hlo_cost_analysis, int64_t scheduler_mem_limit) {
  3248	  tsl::profiler::TraceMe traceme("CreateRematOpts");
  3249	  bool enable_offloading =
  3250	      module.config().debug_options().xla_gpu_enable_host_memory_offloading();
  3251	  std::optional<HloRematerialization::HostMemoryOffloadConfig>
  3252	      offloading_config = std::nullopt;
  3253	  if (enable_offloading) {
  3254	    int64_t host_memory_space_color =
  3255	        static_cast<int64_t>(stream_executor::MemorySpace::kHost);
  3256	    offloading_config =
  3257	        std::make_optional<HloRematerialization::HostMemoryOffloadConfig>(
  3258	            /*host_memory_space=*/host_memory_space_color,
  3259	            /*bandwidth_to_host_bytes_per_second=*/
  3260	            gpu_device_info.memory_bandwidth(),
  3261	            /*bandwidth_from_host_bytes_per_second=*/
  3262	            gpu_device_info.memory_bandwidth());
  3263	  }
  3264	  HloRematerialization::RematerializationModeConfig
  3265	      rematerialization_mode_config(/*recompute=*/true, /*compress=*/true,
  3266	                                    /*host_offload=*/enable_offloading);
  3267	  HloRematerialization::Options options(
  3268	      hlo_cost_analysis, rematerialization_mode_config,
  3269	      // Assume 75% of the total device memory is available for XLA.
  3270	      /*memory_limit_bytes=*/scheduler_mem_limit,
  3271	      /*block_size_limit=*/1, /*block_rematerialization_factor=*/1,
  3272	      /*min_remat_size=*/0, /*compact_shape_function=*/nullptr,
  3273	      /*host_memory_offload_config=*/offloading_config);
  3274	  return options;
  3275	}
  3276	
  3277	absl::Status GpuCompiler::RunPostSchedulingPipelines(
  3278	    HloModule* module, int64_t scheduler_mem_limit,
  3279	    const GpuTopology& gpu_topology, const GpuAliasInfo* alias_info,
  3280	    mlir::MLIRContext* mlir_context) {
  3281	  tsl::profiler::TraceMe traceme("RunPostSchedulingPipelines");
  3282	  ABSL_RETURN_IF_ERROR(RunPostSchedulingCopyInsertion(module, alias_info));
  3283	  {
  3284	    HloPassPipeline post_scheduler_pipeline("post-scheduler-xla-transforms");
  3285	    post_scheduler_pipeline.AddPass<ApplyXlaTransforms>(
  3286	        HloXlaTransform::PipelineStage::kPostScheduler);
  3287	    ABSL_RETURN_IF_ERROR(post_scheduler_pipeline.Run(module).status());
  3288	  }
  3289	  HloPassPipeline main_pipeline("post-scheduling-passes");
  3290	
  3291	  // Pipeline for async -> sync conversion on for non-overlapped async ops.
  3292	  {
  3293	    HloPassPipeline& pipeline =
  3294	        main_pipeline.AddPass<HloPassPipeline>("async-to-sync-converter");
  3295	    pipeline.AddPass<GpuConvertAsyncCollectivesToSync>();
  3296	  }
  3297	
  3298	  // Pipeline rematerialization passes with optional host offloading.
  3299	  HloRematerialization::RematerializationSizes sizes;
  3300	  // `HloCostAnalysis` initialization.
  3301	  const se::DeviceDescription& gpu_device_info =
  3302	      gpu_topology.gpu_target_config().device_description;
  3303	  HloCostAnalysis::Options hlo_cost_analysis_opts =
  3304	      CreateHloAnalysisOpts(*module, gpu_device_info, ShapeSizeBytesFunction());
  3305	  HloCostAnalysis hlo_cost_analysis(hlo_cost_analysis_opts);
  3306	  // `HloRematerialization` options initialization.
  3307	  // `scheduler_mem_limit` cannot directly be reused for HloRematerialization.
  3308	  // It reduces the memory limit by the size of the output, which is done again
  3309	  // in HloRematerialization. So to account for that, add back the size of the
  3310	  // output.
  3311	  int64_t remat_mem_limit = scheduler_mem_limit;
  3312	  ShapeUtil::ForEachSubshape(
  3313	      module->result_shape(),
  3314	      [&](const Shape& subshape, const ShapeIndex& /*index*/) {
  3315	        remat_mem_limit += ShapeSizeBytesFunction()(subshape);
  3316	      });
  3317	  HloRematerialization::Options remat_opts = CreateRematOpts(
  3318	      *module, gpu_device_info, hlo_cost_analysis, remat_mem_limit);
  3319	  {
  3320	    HloPassPipeline& pipeline =
  3321	        main_pipeline.AddPass<HloPassPipeline>("remat-pipeline");
  3322	
  3323	    pipeline.AddPass<HloRematerialization>(remat_opts, sizes);
  3324	    pipeline.AddPass<StreamAttributeAnnotator>(gpu_device_info);
  3325	    pipeline.AddPass<OptimizationBarrierExpander>();
  3326	  }
  3327	
  3328	  // Wrap remaining unfused ops that have no LHLO equivalent in single-op
  3329	  // fusions. This needs to happen after rematerialization, because that
  3330	  // will insert additional copies.
  3331	  {
  3332	    HloPassPipeline& pipeline =
  3333	        main_pipeline.AddPass<HloPassPipeline>("fusion-wrapper");
  3334	    pipeline.AddPass<FusionWrapper>(gpu_device_info);
  3335	  }
  3336	
  3337	  const auto* cuda_cc =
  3338	      gpu_device_info.gpu_compute_capability().cuda_compute_capability();
  3339	  if (cuda_cc != nullptr && cuda_cc->IsAtLeastAmpere()) {
  3340	    // This needs to run after every pass affecting fusions. The last passes
  3341	    // that create new fusions are FusionWrapper and StreamAttributeAnnotator.
  3342	    main_pipeline.AddPass<HloPassPipeline>(FusionDispatchPipeline(
  3343	        gpu_device_info, ShapeSizeBytesFunction(), mlir_context));
  3344	  }
  3345

   948	
   949	absl::StatusOr<ScheduleMetadata> ScheduleGpuModule(
   950	    HloModule* module, int64_t pointer_size,
   951	    const se::DeviceDescription& gpu_device_info,
   952	    mlir::MLIRContext* mlir_context, const GpuAliasInfo* alias_info) {
   953	  tsl::profiler::TraceMe traceme("ScheduleGpuModule");
   954	
   955	  // Tag the module with its 128 bit fingerprint. The fingerprint should include
   956	  // instruction name with ids.
   957	  std::string fingerprint = TagWithFingerprint(module);
   958	  uint64_t memory_limit =
   959	      GetSchedulerMemoryLimit(*module, gpu_device_info, pointer_size);
   960	
   961	  // Module already has a schedule, do nothing.
   962	  if (module->has_schedule()) {
   963	    VLOG(1) << "Module already has a schedule, do nothing.";
   964	    return ScheduleMetadata{memory_limit};
   965	  }
   966	
   967	  // Run the scheduler which minimizes peak memory usage.
   968	  // We need to run it anyway because LHS relies on it.
   969	  // See `xla::LatencyHidingScheduler::Run`.
   970	  ABSL_RETURN_IF_ERROR(RunP2PSchedulePreparation(module));
   971	  int64_t peak_memory_bytes;
   972	  ABSL_ASSIGN_OR_RETURN(HloSchedule schedule,
   973	                   ScheduleGpuModuleWithMemoryScheduler(
   974	                       module, alias_info, pointer_size, &peak_memory_bytes));
   975	  ABSL_RETURN_IF_ERROR(module->set_schedule(std::move(schedule)));
   976	
   977	  bool enable_latency_hiding_scheduler =
   978	      IsLHSEnabled(*module, fingerprint, gpu_device_info);
   979	
   980	  // Run Latency Hiding Scheduler (LHS). It maximizes the compute-communication
   981	  // overlap, potentially at the cost of memory usage.
   982	  if (enable_latency_hiding_scheduler) {
   983	    ABSL_RETURN_IF_ERROR(RunLatencyHidingSchedulerPasses(
   984	        module, pointer_size, fingerprint, memory_limit, gpu_device_info,
   985	        mlir_context, alias_info));
   986	  }
   987	
   988	  return ScheduleMetadata{memory_limit, peak_memory_bytes};
   989	}
   990	
   991	HloInstructionSequence PostProcessSchedule(
   992	    const HloInstructionSequence& input) {
   993	  HloInstructionSequence result = PostprocessorToScheduleSyncCollectives(input);
   994	  return PostprocessorToScheduleAsEarlyOrLateAsPossible(result);
   995	}
   996	
   997	uint64_t GetSchedulerMemoryLimit(const HloModule& module,
   998	                                 const se::DeviceDescription& gpu_device_info,
   999	                                 int pointer_size) {
  1000	  // There is a "base" value which is either specified in HloModuleConfig
  1001	  // (this value should take into account the fact that we need to leave some
  1002	  // memory free for allocations that happen outside of XLA's allocator) or
  1003	  // obtained from GPU device info (we scale down this value to leave some
  1004	  // space for these outside XLA's allocator allocation).
  1005	  //

   196	def _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults(
   197	    *,
   198	    inline_watch_enabled: bool,
   199	    moe_implementation: MoeImplementation | None,
   200	    remat_mode: RematMode,
   201	    processes_per_task: int = 1,
   202	) -> None:
   203	    env_defaults = dict(HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV)
   204	    if processes_per_task > 1:
   205	        # With one process per GPU, the per-process CUPTI sessions collide with each
   206	        # other and with CoreWeave's DCGM, so PGLE cannot profile and its recompile
   207	        # machinery only adds failure modes. Default it off; an explicit env wins.
   208	        env_defaults["JAX_ENABLE_PGLE"] = "false"
   209	    for name, value in env_defaults.items():
   210	        os.environ.setdefault(name, value)
   211	    xla_flags = os.environ.get("XLA_FLAGS", "").split()
   212	    ragged = moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION
   213	    if ragged:
   214	        overlap_limit = RAGGED_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   215	    elif inline_watch_enabled:
   216	        overlap_limit = INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   217	    else:
   218	        overlap_limit = DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   219	    # The scheduler's longer buffer live ranges fit on the ragged transport only once the layer
   220	    # carry leaves HBM. Without that offload its first-step NCCL allocations fail.
   221	    latency_hiding = not ragged or remat_mode == "offload_carry"
   222	    flag_defaults = (
   223	        f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={overlap_limit}",
   224	        f"--xla_gpu_enable_latency_hiding_scheduler={'true' if latency_hiding else 'false'}",
   225	        # The scheduler sizes the single `jit_train_step` temp arena against this percentage of
   226	        # its memory budget, roughly `133.6 GiB x percentage`. The pool holds 138.2 GiB and
   227	        # persistent state occupies 18.1 GiB of it, so an arena above 120.2 GiB cannot be served
   228	        # from pool free space and forces a fresh mapping against the ~17 GiB of physical memory
   229	        # outside the pool. The default 95 asks for 125.7 GiB and fails that way. 85 sizes the
   230	        # arena at 113.6 GiB, leaving enough slack for per-node variation in fragmentation. A
   231	        # lower percentage costs throughput, because a smaller arena makes `HloRematerialization`
   232	        # recompute more of the step.
   233	        "--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85",
   234	        XLA_DISABLE_GPU_COMMAND_BUFFER_FLAG,
   235	    )
   236	    explicit_names = {flag.partition("=")[0] for flag in xla_flags}
   237	    xla_flags.extend(flag for flag in flag_defaults if flag.partition("=")[0] not in explicit_names)
   238	    if remat_mode == "offload_carry":
   239	        # Not overridable, unlike the defaults above, including the scheduler flag it pairs with.
   240	        # Both flags can be inherited from another recipe, but only this one fails silently: a
   241	        # wrong overlap limit corrupts training, while an inherited scheduler setting either
   242	        # aborts in NCCL's first-step allocation and names itself, or runs the slower posture.
   243	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] != XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG]
   244	        xla_flags.append(f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={OFFLOAD_CARRY_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT}")
   245	    if ragged:
   246	        # Unlike the defaults above, these are not overridable. Selecting the host-launched
   247	        # one-shot kernel needs both flags cleared together plus a splits-per-peer count this
   248	        # branch no longer carries, so honoring a partial override would run a configuration
   249	        # nothing here measures. Drop any conflicting entry rather than relying on which
   250	        # occurrence XLA's parser keeps.
   251	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] not in _RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAG_NAMES]
   252	        xla_flags.extend(RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAGS)
   253	    os.environ["XLA_FLAGS"] = " ".join(xla_flags)
   254

   256	def verify_ragged_pjrt() -> None:
   257	    """Raise unless this process runs Marin's patched GPU PJRT plugin."""
   258	    try:
   259	        installed = importlib.metadata.version(PJRT_DISTRIBUTION)
   260	    except importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError as missing:
   261	        raise RuntimeError(
   262	            f"{PJRT_DISTRIBUTION} is not installed, so this process has no GPU PJRT plugin at all."
   263	        ) from missing
   264	    expected_prefix = f"{jax.__version__}+marin."
   265	    if not installed.startswith(expected_prefix):
   266	        raise RuntimeError(
   267	            f"{RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION} needs Marin's patched {PJRT_DISTRIBUTION} "
   268	            f"({expected_prefix}*), found {installed}. The patched wheel is aarch64-only and the "
   269	            "expert MLP is SM100-specialized; run the ragged transport on GB200."
   270	        )

   764	def _compute_diagnostic_watch_stats(params, batch, mp: jmp.Policy, z_loss: float | None, watch_config: WatchConfig):
   765	    (_, _), grads = _loss_and_grads(params, batch, mp, z_loss)
   766	    return compute_watch_stats(
   767	        watch_targets=watch_config.watch_targets,
   768	        include_norms=watch_config.include_norms,
   769	        include_per_parameter_norms=watch_config.include_per_parameter_norms,
   770	        include_histogram=watch_config.include_histograms,
   771	        split_scan_layers=watch_config.split_scan_layers,
   772	        params=params,
   773	        grads=grads,
   774	        model_tree_type=type(params),
   775	    )

   777	
   778	def _make_diagnostic_watch_step(mp: jmp.Policy, *, z_loss_weight: float, watch_config: WatchConfig):
   779	    watch_targets = (
   780	        tuple(t.strip() for t in watch_config.watch_targets.split(","))
   781	        if isinstance(watch_config.watch_targets, str)
   782	        else tuple(watch_config.watch_targets)
   783	    )
   784	    unsupported_targets = set(watch_targets) - {"grads", "params"}
   785	    if unsupported_targets:
   786	        raise ValueError(f"diagnostic watch does not support targets {sorted(unsupported_targets)}")
   787	    diagnostic_watch_config = replace(watch_config, watch_targets=list(watch_targets))
   788	    z_loss = z_loss_weight if z_loss_weight > 0 else None
   789	
   790	    @jax.jit
   791	    def diagnostic_watch_step(params: Transformer, batch, pending_qb_betas: jax.Array):
   792	        params = _apply_qb_betas(params, pending_qb_betas)
   793	        return _compute_diagnostic_watch_stats(params, batch, mp, z_loss, diagnostic_watch_config)
   794	
   795	    return diagnostic_watch_step

   797	
   798	def _make_train_step(
   799	    optimizer: optax.GradientTransformation,
   800	    mp: jmp.Policy,
   801	    *,
   802	    z_loss_weight: float,
   803	    ema_beta: float | None,
   804	    watch_config: WatchConfig | None = None,
   805	    offload_opt_state: bool = False,
   806	    master_param_mode: MasterParamMode = MasterParamMode.DEVICE,
   807	):
   808	    one = jnp.array(1, dtype=jnp.int32)
   809	    z_loss = z_loss_weight if z_loss_weight > 0 else None
   810	    if watch_config is not None:
   811	        if isinstance(watch_config.watch_targets, str):
   812	            watch_targets = tuple(t.strip() for t in watch_config.watch_targets.split(","))
   813	        else:
   814	            watch_targets = tuple(watch_config.watch_targets)
   815	    else:
   816	        watch_targets = ()
   817	
   818	    @functools.partial(jax.jit, donate_argnums=(0,))
   819	    def train_step(state: GrugTrainState, batch):
   820	        # Apply pending QB betas to router biases inside JIT (avoids eager
   821	        # host-side TPU kernel launches that can cause SPMD sync issues).
   822	        qb_params = _apply_qb_betas(state.params, state.pending_qb_betas)
   823	        if ema_beta is not None:
   824	            qb_ema_params = _apply_qb_betas(state.ema_params, state.pending_qb_betas)
   825	        else:
   826	            qb_ema_params = None
   827	
   828	        (loss, summarized_metrics), grads = _loss_and_grads(qb_params, batch, mp, z_loss)
   829	        metrics = {"train/loss": loss, **summarized_metrics}
   830	        opt_state_in = _tree_to_memory_kind(state.opt_state, "device") if offload_opt_state else state.opt_state
   831	        if master_param_mode == MasterParamMode.FP32_PINNED_HOST:
   832	            if state.master_params is None:
   833	                raise ValueError("master_params must be initialized for an FP32 pinned-host master.")
   834	            master_params_in = _tree_to_memory_kind(state.master_params, "device")
   835	            master_params_in = _apply_qb_betas(master_params_in, state.pending_qb_betas)
   836	            master_grads = _FP32_POLICY.cast_to_param(grads)
   837	            updates, opt_state = optimizer.update(master_grads, opt_state_in, master_params_in)
   838	            master_params = optax.apply_updates(master_params_in, updates)
   839	            params = mp.cast_to_param(master_params)
   840	            master_params = _tree_to_memory_kind(master_params, "pinned_host")
   841	        else:
   842	            updates, opt_state = optimizer.update(grads, opt_state_in, qb_params)
   843	            params = optax.apply_updates(qb_params, updates)
   844	            master_params = None
   845	
   846	        if ema_beta is None:
   847	            ema_params = None
   848	        else:
   849	            if qb_ema_params is None:
   850	                raise ValueError("ema_params must be initialized when ema_beta is set.")
   851	            ema_params = jax.tree_util.tree_map(
   852	                lambda old, new: ema_beta * old + (1.0 - ema_beta) * new,
   853	                qb_ema_params,
   854	                params,
   855	            )
   856	
   857	        watch_stats = None
   858	        if watch_config is not None:
   859	            watch_stats = compute_watch_stats(
   860	                watch_targets=watch_targets,
   861	                include_norms=watch_config.include_norms,
   862	                include_per_parameter_norms=watch_config.include_per_parameter_norms,
   863	                include_histogram=watch_config.include_histograms,
   864	                split_scan_layers=watch_config.split_scan_layers,
   865	                params=qb_params,
   866	                grads=grads,
   867	                updates=updates,
   868	                opt_state=opt_state_in,
   869	                model_tree_type=type(state.params),
   870	            )
   871	
   872	        if offload_opt_state:
   873	            opt_state = _tree_to_memory_kind(opt_state, "pinned_host")
   874	
   875	        next_state = dataclasses.replace(
   876	            state,
   877	            step=state.step + one,
   878	            params=params,
   879	            master_params=master_params,
   880	            opt_state=opt_state,
   881	            ema_params=ema_params,
   882	            pending_qb_betas=metrics["qb_beta_per_layer"],
   883	        )
   884	
   885	        return next_state, metrics, watch_stats
   886	
   887	    return train_step
   888

   890	def _run_grug_local(config: GrugRunConfig) -> None:
   891	    """Entry point for the grug template training loop."""
   892	    if config.model.moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION:
   893	        verify_ragged_pjrt()
   894	    if config.tensorstore_cache_bytes is not None:
   895	        set_jagged_array_read_cache_bytes(config.tensorstore_cache_bytes)
   896	
   897	    trainer = config.trainer.trainer
   898	    trainer.initialize()
   899	    levanter.tracker.log_configuration(config)
   900	
   900	
   901	    run_id = trainer.id
   902	    if run_id is None:
   903	        raise ValueError("trainer.id was not initialized")
   904	
   905	    optimizer = config.optimizer.build(trainer.num_train_steps)
   906	    watch_config = trainer.watch
   907	    diagnostic_watch_step = None
   908	    inline_watch_config = watch_config if watch_config.is_enabled else None
   909	    if watch_config.is_enabled and config.trainer.watch_mode == WatchMode.DIAGNOSTIC:
   910	        diagnostic_watch_step = _make_diagnostic_watch_step(
   911	            trainer.mp,
   912	            z_loss_weight=config.trainer.z_loss_weight,
   913	            watch_config=watch_config,
   914	        )
   915	        inline_watch_config = None
   916	    train_step = _make_train_step(
   917	        optimizer,
   918	        trainer.mp,
   919	        z_loss_weight=config.trainer.z_loss_weight,
   920	        ema_beta=config.trainer.ema_beta,
   921	        watch_config=inline_watch_config,
   922	        offload_opt_state=config.trainer.offload_opt_state,
   923	        master_param_mode=config.trainer.master_param_mode,
   924	    )
   925	
   926	    data_key, model_key = jax.random.split(jax.random.PRNGKey(trainer.seed), 2)
   927	    if config.trainer.data_seed is not None:
   928	        data_key = jax.random.PRNGKey(config.trainer.data_seed)
   929	
   930	    # Grug uses raw PartitionSpecs rather than Trainer's logical axis mapping.
   931	    # Keep the mesh compact so the batch pspec derived by `_batch_spec(mesh)` spans slices directly.
   932	    # replica_axis_size=None lets compact_grug_mesh default to jax.process_count() (full
   933	    # cross-slice replication); set it to 1 on GrugTrainerConfig for cross-slice FSDP.
   934	    mesh = compact_grug_mesh(
   935	        expert_axis_size=config.trainer.expert_axis_size,
   936	        replica_axis_size=config.trainer.replica_axis_size,
   937	    )
   938	    # Armed before the state is built or restored. The watchdog's step and process deadlines only
   939	    # arm once a step reports progress, so its startup deadline is the only thing bounding a stall
   940	    # in initialization, checkpoint restore, cache construction or compilation.
   941	    progress_watchdog = trainer.progress_watchdog.create(process_index=jax.process_index())
   942	
   943	    checkpointer = trainer.checkpointer.create(run_id) if config.trainer.save_checkpoints else None
   944	    dashboard = (
   945	        TrainingDashboard(config, checkpointer.request_checkpoint, run_id) if checkpointer is not None else nullcontext()
   946	    )
   947	    with set_mesh(mesh), dashboard:
   948	        batch_schedule = trainer.batch_schedule
   949	
   950	        @jax.jit
   951	        def _init_state(model_rng):
   952	            return initial_state(
   953	                config.model,
   954	                optimizer=optimizer,
   955	                mp=trainer.mp,
   956	                key=model_rng,
   957	                ema_beta=config.trainer.ema_beta,
   958	                offload_opt_state=config.trainer.offload_opt_state,
   959	                master_param_mode=config.trainer.master_param_mode,
   960	            )
   961	
   962	        state = _init_state(model_key)
   963	        released_initial_state = trainer.load_checkpoint is not False and not trainer.allow_partial_checkpoint
   964	        if released_initial_state:
   965	            state = restore_template_from(state)
   966	
   967	        state = restore_grug_state_from_checkpoint(
   968	            state,
   969	            checkpoint_search_paths=trainer.checkpoint_search_paths(run_id),
   970	            load_checkpoint_setting=trainer.load_checkpoint,
   971	            mesh=mesh,
   972	            allow_partial=trainer.allow_partial_checkpoint,
   973	            template_for_candidate=lambda candidate: template_for_candidate_layout(
   974	                state, candidate, config.trainer.master_param_mode
   975	            ),
   976	        )
   977	        if config.trainer.master_param_mode == MasterParamMode.DEVICE:
   978	            state = take_master_as_params(state)
   979	        if released_initial_state and any(isinstance(leaf, jax.ShapeDtypeStruct) for leaf in jax.tree.leaves(state)):
   980	            state = _init_state(model_key)
   981	        dump_grug_state_sharding_run_artifact(
   982	            state,
   983	            log_dir=trainer.log_dir,
   984	            run_id=run_id,
   985	            path_override=config.trainer.sharding_dump_path,
   986	        )
   987	
   988	        levanter.tracker.log_summary({"parameter_count": parameter_count(state.params)})
   989	
   990	        train_dataset = None
   991	        train_loader = None
   992	        if config.trainer.training_data_mode == TrainingDataMode.MIXTURE:
   993	            train_dataset = build_train_dataset(
   994	                config.data,
   995	                max_seq_len=config.model.max_seq_len,
   996	                batch_schedule=batch_schedule,
   997	                key=data_key,
   998	            )
   999	            train_loader = build_train_loader(
  1000	                train_dataset,
  1001	                batch_schedule=batch_schedule,
  1002	                mesh=mesh,
  1003	            )
  1004	
  1005	        flops_per_example, flops_summary = _compute_flops(model_config=config.model)
  1006	        levanter.tracker.log_summary(flops_summary)
  1007	
  1008	        eval_cfg = config.eval
  1009	        evaluator = None
  1010	        dropless_evaluator = None
  1011	        dropless_eval_mesh = None
  1012	        if eval_cfg is not None:
  1013	            evaluator = build_tagged_evaluator(
  1014	                data_config=config.data,
  1015	                max_seq_len=config.model.max_seq_len,
  1016	                mesh=mesh,
  1017	                eval_cfg=eval_cfg,
  1018	                mp=trainer.mp,
  1019	            )
  1020	            # Expert-parallel runs drop tokens over capacity; a second evaluator scores the same
  1021	            # weights dropless under the local backend on an expert-collapsed mesh (expert folded
  1022	            # into `data`), which the local backend requires. FSDP runs already have expert=1.
  1023	            if eval_cfg.dropless_eval and mesh.shape["expert"] > 1:

  1025	                    expert_axis_size=1,
  1026	                    replica_axis_size=mesh.shape["replica_dcn"],
  1027	                    model_axis_size=mesh.shape["model"],
  1028	                )
  1029	                # Build under the eval mesh so every constant the evaluator captures at construction
  1030	                # (e.g. `log2e`, the byte-per-token table, output shardings) is bound to the eval mesh

    58	class _ExpertMlp(Protocol):
    59	    """Runs the expert MLP over a receiver buffer laid out expert-major.
    60	
    61	    Implementations take both views of the buffer's group sizes: the physical sizes, which charge
    62	    trailing padding to the last expert, and the active sizes, which count only received rows.
    63	    Which one a kernel reads depends on whether it covers the whole buffer or works from segment
    64	    boundaries, so both are always passed and a kernel discards the one it does not use.
    65	    """
    66	
    67	    def __call__(
    68	        self,
    69	        x_dispatch: Float[Array, "C H"],
    70	        moe_w13_local: Float[Array, "Echunk H I2"],
    71	        moe_w2_local: Float[Array, "Echunk I H"],
    72	        physical_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    73	        active_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    74	        activation_fn: Callable[[jax.Array], jax.Array],
    75	    ) -> Float[Array, "C H"]: ...
    76	
    77	
    78	def _ragged_dot_expert_mlp(
    79	    x_dispatch: Float[Array, "C H"],
    80	    moe_w13_local: Float[Array, "Echunk H I2"],
    81	    moe_w2_local: Float[Array, "Echunk I H"],
    82	    physical_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    83	    active_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    84	    activation_fn: Callable[[jax.Array], jax.Array],
    85	) -> Float[Array, "C H"]:
    20	
    21	# `JobRequest.priority` is the Iris priority band as a bare int. INHERIT is Iris's own default.
    22	INHERIT_PRIORITY = priority_band_value("inherit")
    23	
    24	# Runtime-tuning env vars forwarded from the dispatcher to the train tasks.
    25	# Iris tasks don't inherit the submitter's shell, so anything the launcher was
    26	# given (e.g. `iris job run -e XLA_FLAGS ...`) must be re-exported explicitly.
    27	# JAX_PLATFORMS is excluded: the dispatcher runs CPU-only and its value must
    28	# not leak onto accelerator tasks.
    29	_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES = ("XLA_", "LIBTPU_INIT_ARGS", "NCCL_", "JAX_", "MALLOC_")
    30	# TF_CPP_* control XLA's C++ logging; measurement harnesses forward them to read compile-time
    31	# scheduler/remat evidence (memory limits, remat counts) out of the task logs.
    32	_FORWARDED_ENV_NAMES = ("LD_PRELOAD", "TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL", "TF_CPP_VMODULE")
    33	_FORWARDED_ENV_EXCLUDE = ("JAX_PLATFORMS",)
    34	
    35	
    36	def _forwarded_env_vars() -> dict[str, str]:
    37	    return {
    38	        k: v
    39	        for k, v in os.environ.items()
    40	        if (k in _FORWARDED_ENV_NAMES or k.startswith(_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES)) and k not in _FORWARDED_ENV_EXCLUDE
    41	    }
    42	
    43	
    44	def _safe_job_suffix(run_id: str) -> str:
    45	    """Sanitize run IDs into Fray/Iris-safe job-name suffixes."""
    46	    return re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9_.-]+", "-", run_id)
    47	
    48	
    49	def dispatch_grug_training_run(
    50	    *,
    51	    run_id: str,
    52	    config: ConfigT,
    53	    local_entrypoint: Callable[[ConfigT], None],
    54	    resources: ResourceConfig,
    55	    max_retries_failure: int = 3,
    56	    max_task_failures: int = 10,
    57	    processes_per_task: int = 1,
    58	    priority: int = INHERIT_PRIORITY,
    59	) -> None:
    60	    """Submit a grug train entrypoint through Fray and wait for completion.
    61	
    62	    ``INHERIT_PRIORITY`` takes the submitting job's band, or ``interactive`` when the submitter is
    63	    not itself an Iris job -- which is the case for a launcher run from a dev box.
    64	
    65	    ``max_retries_failure`` is the per-task retry budget and ``max_task_failures`` is the
    66	    cumulative one. The job fails when either is exhausted, so raise the two together: a large
    67	    per-task budget under a small cumulative one still ends the job at the cumulative limit.
    68	    """
    69	    safe_run_id = _safe_job_suffix(run_id)
    70	    env_vars = resolve_training_env(base_env=_forwarded_env_vars(), resources=resources)
    71	    request = JobRequest(
    72	        name=f"grug-train-{safe_run_id}",
    73	        entrypoint=Entrypoint.from_callable(local_entrypoint, args=[config]),
    74	        resources=resources,
    75	        environment=create_environment(env_vars=env_vars, extras=extras_for_resources(resources)),
    76	        max_retries_failure=max_retries_failure,
    77	        max_task_failures=max_task_failures,
    78	        processes_per_task=processes_per_task,
    79	        priority=priority,
    80	    )
    81	    logger.info("Dispatching grug training via Fray: %s", request.name)
    82	    job = current_client().submit(request)
    83	    job.wait(raise_on_failure=True)
    38	  echo "refusing to submit from a dirty tree (set ALLOW_DIRTY=1 to override):" >&2
    39	  echo "$dirty" >&2
    40	  exit 1
    41	fi
    42	
    43	# A flag-based treatment must be deliberate: an ARM_XLA_FLAGS value leaking from the supervisor
    44	# shell into a "control" draw silently runs the treatment and records nothing -- the null delta
    45	# then reads as "dead lever". Controls run with TREATMENT unset and ARM_XLA_FLAGS empty.
    46	if [ -n "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-}" ] && [ -z "${TREATMENT:-}" ]; then
    47	  echo "ARM_XLA_FLAGS is set but TREATMENT=1 is not; refusing (is this a contaminated control?)" >&2
    48	  exit 1
    49	fi
    50	
    51	# One RID/VERSION per submission, ever: a reused RID merges W&B histories, resurrects the
    52	# leader-populated compile cache (clique-deadlock recipe), and can vacuously reuse artifacts.
    53	if [ -f "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv" ] && awk -F'\t' -v rid="$RID" -v ver="$VERSION" \
    54	    'NR>1 && ($1==rid || $3==ver) {found=1} END {exit !found}' "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv"; then
    55	  echo "RID ${RID} or VERSION ${VERSION} already appears in arms.tsv; pick fresh ones" >&2
    56	  exit 1
    57	fi
    58	
    59	PRIORITY="${PRIORITY:-production}"
    60	# Covers Kueue queue wait PLUS the run: same-band gangs queue FIFO behind whatever holds the
    61	# rack, and the coordinator's timeout clock runs while its child sits in the gate. Occupancy
    62	# after admission is intrinsically bounded by NUM_STEPS (~30 steps past restore); the watchdog
    63	# enforces the <1h-runtime rule from admission.
    64	ARM_TIMEOUT="${ARM_TIMEOUT:-28800}"
    65	NUM_STEPS="${NUM_STEPS:-30030}"
    66	TRAINING_DATA="${TRAINING_DATA:-mixture}"
    67	MASTER_PARAMS="${MASTER_PARAMS:-device}"
    68	MOE_IMPL="${MOE_IMPL:-ragged_all_to_all}"
    69	RESTORE_FROM="${RESTORE_FROM:-s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000}"
    70	RESTORE_ARGS=()
    71	if [ -n "$RESTORE_FROM" ]; then
    72	  RESTORE_ARGS=(--restore-from "$RESTORE_FROM")
    73	fi
    74	# The learning-rate schedule's length; fixed across arms so losses stay comparable.
    75	SCHEDULE_STEPS=4470000
    76	
    77	read -r -a EXTRA <<<"${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-}"
    78	
    79	cd "$REPO"
    80	uv run iris --config lib/iris/config/marin.yaml job run \
    81	  --no-wait --enable-extra-resources \
    82	  --target-cluster cw-us-east-08a --priority "${PRIORITY}" \
    83	  --cpu 2 --memory 8GB --disk 32GB --timeout "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" \
    84	  --job-name "${RID}-coord" \
    85	  -e IRIS_USER mwittmann \
    86	  -e WANDB_API_KEY "${WANDB_API_KEY}" \
    87	  -e WANDB_PROJECT marin_moe \
    88	  -e MARIN_PREFIX s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin \
    89	  -e IRIS_PORT_JAX 32703 \
    90	  -e AWS_MAX_ATTEMPTS 25 -e AWS_RETRY_MODE adaptive \
    91	  -e JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR "s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/tmp/ttl=30d/jaxcache/${RID}" \
    92	  -e XLA_FLAGS "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-}" \
    93	  -e TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL 0 \
    94	  -e TF_CPP_VMODULE "hlo_rematerialization=1" \
    95	  -- python -m experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics \
    96	     --run-id "${RID}" \
    97	     --dp-racks 1 --num-steps "${NUM_STEPS}" --schedule-steps "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" \
    98	     --capacity-factor 1.15 \
    99	     --moe-implementation "${MOE_IMPL}" \
   100	     --processes-per-task 4 \
   101	     --master-params "${MASTER_PARAMS}" \
   102	     --training-data "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
   103	     --profile-steps "${PROFILE_STEPS:-0}" --profile-start-step "${PROFILE_START_STEP:-30021}" \
   104	     --watch-interval 0 --eval-every 0 \
   105	     --no-save-checkpoints \
   106	     "${RESTORE_ARGS[@]}" \
   107	     "${EXTRA[@]}" \
   108	     --version "${VERSION}" --run >"${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" 2>&1 \
   109	  || { echo "submit FAILED, tail of ${RID}-submit.log:" >&2; tail -20 "${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" >&2; exit 1; }
   110	
   111	printf '%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n' \
   112	  "${RID}" "$(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD)" "${VERSION}" "${MOE_IMPL}" "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
   113	  "${MASTER_PARAMS}" "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" "${RESTORE_FROM:-none}" \
   114	  "${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none}" "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-none}" >> "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv"
   115	
   116	echo "submitted ${RID} at ${PRIORITY} priority (timeout ${ARM_TIMEOUT}s, commit $(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD), data ${TRAINING_DATA}, extra: ${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none}, xla_flags: ${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-none})"

exec

codex
The exact fork adds another attribution hazard: both flags are global transformations, not selectors for the identified nine copies or the one carry loop. The async wrapper visits every eligible ≥1 GB D2D copy in every non-fusion computation, and the host-offload flag pipelines every structurally eligible host move. So “the expected target moved” is necessary but not sufficient; the compile evidence must enumerate all transformed sites and bytes.

/etc/profiles/per-user/marin/bin/bash -lc 'gh pr view 46106 --repo openxla/xla --json number,title,body,url,state,mergedAt,files' in /home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop

{"body":"## Background\n\n`DeviceToDeviceCopyThunk` issues a memcpy on the main compute stream.  Because the copy and subsequent kernels share the same stream they are serialized — they cannot overlap even though CUDA/ROCm supports copy–compute concurrency on distinct streams.\n\nXLA already has the full async machinery in place for collectives and dynamic-slice copy fusions: `AsyncStartThunk` dispatches nested thunks onto an auxiliary compute stream and records a completion event; `AsyncDoneThunk` makes the main stream wait for that event; the latency-hiding scheduler places compute kernels between start and done.  Two gaps blocked D2D copies from using this path:\n\n1. `EmitCopyStartThunk` returned `InternalError` for `kCopyStart` instructions with both source and destination in device memory.\n2. There was no HLO pass to convert plain `kCopy` to `kCopyStart`/`kCopyDone` before scheduling.\n\n## Changes\n\n### Commit 1 — Fix `EmitCopyStartThunk` for D2D (`xla/service/gpu/thunk_emitter.cc`)\n\nChanged the guard from \"error when both sides are the same memory kind\" to \"error only on unsupported H2H\".  Added a D2D branch that creates a `DeviceToDeviceCopyThunk`.  The async-wrapping path (`AsyncStartThunk`, synchronous fallback, `AsyncExecution` registration) is unchanged.\n\n### Commit 2 — `GpuCopyAsyncWrapper` pass\n\nNew pre-scheduling `HloModulePass` (`xla/backends/gpu/transforms/gpu_copy_async_wrapper.{h,cc}`) that converts eligible `kCopy` instructions to `kCopyStart`/`kCopyDone` pairs.\n\n**Eligibility criteria:**\n- Both source and destination in device memory (no host-memory-space annotation).\n- Source and destination layouts match (a layout-changing copy is a transpose in disguise, not a memcpy). `EmitCopyStartThunk` re-checks this with a `TF_RET_CHECK` in the D2D branch.\n- Transfer size ≥ `xla_gpu_async_copy_min_bytes` (default 64 KiB).\n- `xla_gpu_enable_async_device_to_device_copy` is true (default **false** for safe rollout).\n\nControl dependencies of the original copy are preserved: predecessors move to `copy-start`, successors to `copy-done`.\n\n**End-to-end data flow once the flag is enabled:**\n```\nkCopy (D2D, large)\n  → GpuCopyAsyncWrapper\n  → kCopyStart + kCopyDone\n  → ExecutionStreamAssignment assigns ComputationStreamId(N)   [unchanged]\n  → EmitCopyStartThunk: AsyncStartThunk[DeviceToDeviceCopyThunk]  [fixed]\n  → LHS schedules compute kernels in the overlap window          [unchanged]\n  → AsyncDoneThunk re-synchronizes the main stream               [unchanged]\n```\n\n**Nothing changed** in `AsyncStartThunk`, `AsyncDoneThunk`, `AsyncExecution`, `execution_stream_assignment.cc`, `gpu_latency_hiding_scheduler.cc`, or `command_buffer_conversion_pass.cc` — existing machinery already handles `kCopyStart` correctly.\n\n## New proto fields (`xla/xla.proto`)\n- `xla_gpu_enable_async_device_to_device_copy` (bool, default false) — feature gate.\n- `xla_gpu_async_copy_min_bytes` (int64, default 65536) — size threshold. Both flags are registered in `debug_options_flags.cc` and settable via `XLA_FLAGS`.\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] `xla/backends/gpu/transforms:gpu_copy_async_wrapper_test` — unit tests for the pass: conversion of large D2D copy, flag gate, size threshold (explicit and 64 KiB default), layout-changing copy exclusion, control-dependency preservation, idempotency. All 8 tests pass.\n- [x] Thunk emitter integration test: verify `kCopyStart`/`kCopyDone` HLO lowered to `AsyncStartThunk[DeviceToDeviceCopyThunk]` + `AsyncDoneThunk`.\n- [x] End-to-end: enable the flag, run a compute + large D2D copy module, verify numerical correctness and GPU timeline overlap.\n","files":[{"path":"xla/backends/gpu/transforms/BUILD","additions":30,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/backends/gpu/transforms/gpu_copy_async_wrapper.cc","additions":116,"deletions":0,"changeType":"ADDED"},{"path":"xla/backends/gpu/transforms/gpu_copy_async_wrapper.h","additions":60,"deletions":0,"changeType":"ADDED"},{"path":"xla/backends/gpu/transforms/gpu_copy_async_wrapper_test.cc","additions":239,"deletions":0,"changeType":"ADDED"},{"path":"xla/debug_options_flags.cc","additions":8,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/BUILD","additions":1,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc","additions":3,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/thunk_emitter.cc","additions":22,"deletions":9,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/xla.proto","additions":7,"deletions":1,"changeType":"MODIFIED"}],"mergedAt":null,"number":46106,"state":"CLOSED","title":"[XLA:GPU] Supporting Async D2D copies for DeviceToDeviceCopyThunk","url":"https://github.com/openxla/xla/pull/46106"}

{"body":"📝 Summary of Changes\r\n1. Add host offloading support to collective pipeliner to enable memory optimization for collective operations by offloading intermediate results to host memory.\r\n\r\n2. Add dynamic variable detection for CollectivePipeliner-transformed loops to enable FusionDynamicMemcpyRewriter optimization, allowing better memory management for dynamically shaped computations.\r\n\r\n🎯 Justification\r\nWhen host memory offloading is enabled, activations are copied asynchronously; device to host in the forward pass and back for the backward pass. These copies often happen at loop boundaries, limiting overlap with compute. This PR adds host offloading support to collective pipeliner so that we can move these copies to nearby iterations to reduce dependencies and improve overlap. This feature is enabled via `xla_gpu_enable_pipelined_host_offloading` flag. \r\n\r\n🚀 Kind of Contribution\r\n⚡️ Performance Improvement, ✨ New Feature\r\n\r\n📊 Benchmark (for Performance Improvements)\r\nThe changes in this PR are gated by a flag. The HLOs under `xla/tools/benchmarks/hlo` don't affect offloading behavior, so we shouldn't expect any impact.\r\n\r\n🧪 Unit Tests:\r\nAdded unit tests.\r\n\r\n🧪 Execution Tests:\r\nSaw ~12% speedup on GB200 with llama3-8b, fsdp=8 using MLP offloading plus collective pipelining, compared to just offloading.","files":[{"path":"docs/flags_guidance.md","additions":1,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/backends/gpu/codegen/copy_test.cc","additions":67,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/debug_options_flags.cc","additions":10,"deletions":3,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/BUILD","additions":2,"deletions":1,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/collective_pipeliner.cc","additions":64,"deletions":30,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/collective_pipeliner.h","additions":12,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/collective_pipeliner_test.cc","additions":213,"deletions":3,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/BUILD","additions":2,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc","additions":105,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/ir_emission_utils.cc","additions":41,"deletions":11,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/ir_emission_utils_test.cc","additions":159,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/transforms/collectives/gpu_collective_combiner_utils_test.cc","additions":2,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/transforms/double_buffer_loop_unrolling.cc","additions":7,"deletions":8,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/gpu/transforms/double_buffer_loop_unrolling_test.cc","additions":90,"deletions":1,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/host_offload_utils.cc","additions":90,"deletions":1,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/service/host_offload_utils.h","additions":4,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/xla.proto","additions":3,"deletions":1,"changeType":"MODIFIED"},{"path":"xla/xla_data.proto","additions":4,"deletions":0,"changeType":"MODIFIED"}],"mergedAt":null,"number":32297,"state":"CLOSED","title":"[XLA:GPU]    716	def _drop_metrics(
   717	    dropped_assignments: jax.Array,
   718	    sender_dropped_assignments: jax.Array,
   719	    receiver_dropped_assignments: jax.Array,
   720	    *,
   721	    batch_size: int,
   722	    sequence_length: int,
   723	    top_k: int,
   724	    num_layers: int,
   725	) -> dict[str, int | float]:
   726	    # Per-layer int32 counts summed over layers in int64 on the host: the global totals exceed int32 at
   727	    # large batch (jax_enable_x64 is off, so an in-device sum would overflow), and float32 would round them.
   728	    def _sum_int64(per_layer: jax.Array) -> int:
   729	        return int(np.asarray(per_layer).astype(np.int64).sum())
   730	
   731	    dropped_assignments_host = _sum_int64(dropped_assignments)
   732	    sender_dropped_assignments_host = _sum_int64(sender_dropped_assignments)
   733	    receiver_dropped_assignments_host = _sum_int64(receiver_dropped_assignments)
   734	    if dropped_assignments_host != sender_dropped_assignments_host + receiver_dropped_assignments_host:
   735	        raise ValueError("total dropped assignments must equal sender plus receiver dropped assignments")
   736	    total_assignments = batch_size * sequence_length * top_k * num_layers
   737	    receiver_assignments = total_assignments - sender_dropped_assignments_host
   738	    return {
   739	        "moe/dropped_assignments": dropped_assignments_host,
   740	        "moe/drop_fraction": dropped_assignments_host / total_assignments,
   741	        "moe/sender_dropped_assignments": sender_dropped_assignments_host,
   742	        "moe/sender_drop_fraction": sender_dropped_assignments_host / total_assignments,
   743	        "moe/receiver_dropped_assignments": receiver_dropped_assignments_host,
   744	        "moe/receiver_drop_fraction": receiver_dropped_assignments_host / total_assignments,
   745	        "moe/receiver_drop_fraction_of_received": receiver_dropped_assignments_host / max(receiver_assignments, 1),
   746	    }
   747

   749	def _loss_and_grads(params, batch, mp: jmp.Policy, z_loss: float | None):
   750	    def loss_fn(model):
   751	        compute_params = mp.cast_to_compute(model)
   752	        return compute_params.next_token_loss(

   759	        )
   760

H10 is stop-ship as specified. H9 is conditionally runnable only after compile-level engagement and fixed-stack attribution rules are added.

## Ranked findings

1. **CRITICAL — H10’s 15-step loss gate can miss the documented race.**

   The scored window is restore steps `+5..+19`, while the known offload/LHS/overlap corruption presented on the first restored step ([DESIGN.md:95](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:95), [train.py:94](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:94), [PR #8753](https://github.com/marin-community/marin/pull/8753)). H10 nevertheless gates only on the “scored loss series” ([LOGBOOK.md:300](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/LOGBOOK.md:300)).

   The logged loss is also a cross-shard mean: `train.py` requests `reduction="mean"` and the loss implementation `psum`s local sums before division ([train.py:749](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:749), [loss.py:130](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/loss.py:130)). An intermittent one-rank finite error can therefore be diluted below the calibrated global `1.05e-4` maximum.

   Required additional gate:

   - Compare every step `+0..+29`, including the first restored step, across at least two independently cold-compiled H10 draws.
   - Persist rank-indexed local loss sum/denominator plus per-rank nonfinite, gradient/update norm, or low-collision state-digest observables. Gate on the maximum rank delta, not their mean.
   - Calibrate those rank-local observables from C-C runs; the existing global-loss band cannot be reused unchanged.
   - A dedicated non-scored checksum/sentinel on the reloaded carry immediately before its consumer would be stronger still.

   The fork’s host-pipelining tests do not close this gap: they parse unverified HLO and assert structural transformation/dynamic-variable indices, not numerical equivalence, alias safety, or distributed execution ([collective_pipeliner_test.cc:5746-5787](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner_test.cc#L5746-L5787), [collective_pipeliner_test.cc:5852-5893](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner_test.cc#L5852-L5893)).

2. **CRITICAL — DESIGN contains a stale decision rule that permits both false keeps and false dead-levers.**

   The calibrated rule correctly requires ≥2 treatment draws, ≥2 bracketing controls, run medians, and a current `0.16` MFU bar ([DESIGN.md:109](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:109)). But the bottom summary still says a single same-night pair and `>0.15`, and says any guard failure discards the arm ([DESIGN.md:235](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:235)). That also contradicts the calibrated rule that a small fidelity exceedance triggers another draw/investigation rather than automatic rejection ([DESIGN.md:125](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:125)).

   Treat lines 237–239 as invalid. Otherwise an operator can keep from one lucky pair or kill a lever on a small, investigable guard excursion.

3. **HIGH — H9 is a global ≥1 GB copy transformation, not an arm targeting only the nine identified copies.**

   `GpuCopyAsyncWrapper` visits every non-fusion computation and wraps every eligible D2D copy above the threshold ([gpu_copy_async_wrapper.cc:79-113](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/backends/gpu/transforms/gpu_copy_async_wrapper.cc#L79-L113)). Thus the nine `copy.7xx` sites and their 1.85–3.22 GB sizes are not an upper bound. Other ≥1 GB copies could dominate memory or attribution.

   For the known sites alone, the async-compute limit of two allows roughly two largest copies—about 6 GB—live concurrently ([gpu_latency_hiding_scheduler.cc:689-718](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_latency_hiding_scheduler.cc#L689-L718)). That is close to the entire slop-85→90 scheduler-budget increase, approximately 6.6 GiB. Although the nominal pool headroom is `138.2 - 116.57 = 21.63 GiB`, the slop-85 scheduler/remat limit already binds; H9 can consume H6’s headroom through longer live ranges without directly exhausting the allocator.

   This fork’s explicit large-buffer scheduler fencing is disabled by default ([debug_options_flags.cc:516-522](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L516-L522)). Rematerialization runs after LHS scheduling, so the existing remat-count log is the right confound detector ([gpu_compiler.cc:2811-2824](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc#L2811-L2824), [gpu_compiler.cc:3298-3325](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc#L3298-L3325)).

   An H9 compile/OOM or remat increase means “negative interaction with this memory budget,” not “async copy is a dead lever.”

4. **HIGH — Current engagement checks do not fully separate no-op, engaged-null, and collateral transformation.**

   For H9, exact target stream placement is useful, but “compute-stream MemcpyD2D → ~0” does not enumerate other copies wrapped by the global pass or prove a useful start/done overlap window. For H10, reduced exposed-copy time can result from changed copy count or compensating waits and does not prove both forward and backward loop rotations.

   Profile-free compile observables should be mandatory:

   - **H9:** dump/count post-`gpu-copy-async-wrapper` `copy-start` sites, byte-size histogram, exact target-site count, total wrapped-site count, and scheduled start→done distance. Record remat count and compiled temp-memory estimate.
   - **H10:** use `TF_CPP_VMODULE=collective_pipeliner=1,hlo_pass_pipeline=3`; record forward/backward transformed-loop and transformed-instruction counts whose `MoveInfo` contains `MoveToHost`/`MoveToDevice`. The pass already logs these counters ([collective_pipeliner.cc:3503-3542](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner.cc#L3503-L3542), [collective_pipeliner.cc:3606-3609](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner.cc#L3606-L3609)).
   - Log to W&B summary: exact canonical `XLA_FLAGS`, PJRT version, optimized-HLO fingerprint, transform counts/bytes, start→done schedule distance, remat count, and peak HBM.

   Classification should be:

   - `0` target transforms: predicate-miss/no-op; no dead-lever conclusion.
   - Target transforms plus positive schedule movement/aux-stream placement, MFU below bar: engaged-but-null on this stack.
   - Target transforms plus remat/stall/memory growth: engaged but confounded/negative interaction.

5. **HIGH — Slop-90 cannot enter the control stack mid-comparison without invalidating attribution.**

   H6 directly changes the memory budget H9 consumes, and H9/H10 share async-compute scheduling resources with existing offload operations. The current “keeps become the new control” language ([DESIGN.md:237](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:237)) is insufficient for this interaction.

   Correct ordering:

   - Finish the complete 2C/2T H6 decision before starting H9/H10, or freeze slop-85 until every draw for the current hypothesis finishes.
   - Never pool controls or treatment draws across slop-stack changes.
   - If slop-90 becomes control, prior H9/H10 results remain claims only about slop-85; rerun fresh bracketing controls and treatments on slop-90.
   - Because the H6 budget increase is approximately the H9 two-copy live-range increase, use an H6×H9 2×2 comparison or back-ablate H9 from the final slop-90 stack.
   - If H9 and H10 both keep, the planned joint back-ablation remains mandatory because they share the async-compute limit ([LOGBOOK.md:310](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/LOGBOOK.md:310)).

6. **MEDIUM — H10 can already be active in a nominal control through optimization effort.**

   The exact fork enables host-offload pipelining when the flag is true **or** optimization effort is O1+ ([gpu_compiler.cc:1213-1245](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc#L1213-L1245)). JAX 0.11.1 nominally defaults `jax_optimization_level` to `UNKNOWN` ([config.py:2262](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/jax/_src/config.py:2262)), but the dispatcher forwards arbitrary `JAX_*` environment variables ([dispatch.py:24](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/dispatch.py:24)).

   `JAX_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL` must therefore be asserted unset/`UNKNOWN` in both control and treatment and logged. Otherwise H10 treatment can be an exact control despite the flag being present.

7. **MEDIUM — Exact fork source is verified; exact on-rack binary remains UNVERIFIED.**

   The lock pins the expected wheel URL and SHA256 ([uv.lock:2631](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/uv.lock:2631)), and the fetched fork tag resolves to full commit `c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92`. Both flags are registered there, defaulting to `-1` and `false` respectively ([debug_options_flags.cc:578](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L578), [debug_options_flags.cc:2491](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L2491), [debug_options_flags.cc:3503](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L3503)).

   However, `verify_ragged_pjrt()` accepts any `0.11.1+marin.*` build rather than requiring `c9526e8c0272` ([train.py:256](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:256)). The exact installed on-rack artifact and file hash were not available, so they remain **UNVERIFIED**.

## Per-flag failure surface

| Arm | Compile/startup failure | Silent no-op | Engaged runtime failure |
|---|---|---|---|
| H9 async D2D | Wrong wheel: unknown-flag fatal. Exact wheel: async-HLO creation, verifier, scheduler/remat failure, or compile-time inflation. | No eligible ≥1 GB same-layout D2D copies; generic “pass ran” is insufficient. | Longer live ranges cause extra remat, overlap suppression, fragmentation/OOM, or memory-bandwidth contention. |
| H10 pipelined offload | Wrong wheel: unknown-flag fatal. Exact wheel: CollectivePipeliner/while rewrite or verifier failure. | Predicate miss, unsupported chain/control dependency, or control already enabled by O1+. | Finite silent stale reload, intermittent/rank-local corruption, added peeled/prefetched-buffer memory, or compensating waits. |

Finally, `_apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults` does **not** strip or force either proposed flag. It preserves explicit flags at [train.py:211](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:211); only the collective-overlap name is force-replaced for `offload_carry`, and only the two ragged-required names are replaced at [train.py:238](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:238). Overlap remains correctly pinned to 1.

## Ranked findings

1. **CRITICAL — H10’s 15-step loss gate can miss the documented race.**

   The scored window is restore steps `+5..+19`, while the known offload/LHS/overlap corruption presented on the first restored step ([DESIGN.md:95](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:95), [train.py:94](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:94), [PR #8753](https://github.com/marin-community/marin/pull/8753)). H10 nevertheless gates only on the “scored loss series” ([LOGBOOK.md:300](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/LOGBOOK.md:300)).

   The logged loss is also a cross-shard mean: `train.py` requests `reduction="mean"` and the loss implementation `psum`s local sums before division ([train.py:749](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:749), [loss.py:130](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/loss.py:130)). An intermittent one-rank finite error can therefore be diluted below the calibrated global `1.05e-4` maximum.

   Required additional gate:

   - Compare every step `+0..+29`, including the first restored step, across at least two independently cold-compiled H10 draws.
   - Persist rank-indexed local loss sum/denominator plus per-rank nonfinite, gradient/update norm, or low-collision state-digest observables. Gate on the maximum rank delta, not their mean.
   - Calibrate those rank-local observables from C-C runs; the existing global-loss band cannot be reused unchanged.
   - A dedicated non-scored checksum/sentinel on the reloaded carry immediately before its consumer would be stronger still.

   The fork’s host-pipelining tests do not close this gap: they parse unverified HLO and assert structural transformation/dynamic-variable indices, not numerical equivalence, alias safety, or distributed execution ([collective_pipeliner_test.cc:5746-5787](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner_test.cc#L5746-L5787), [collective_pipeliner_test.cc:5852-5893](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner_test.cc#L5852-L5893)).

2. **CRITICAL — DESIGN contains a stale decision rule that permits both false keeps and false dead-levers.**

   The calibrated rule correctly requires ≥2 treatment draws, ≥2 bracketing controls, run medians, and a current `0.16` MFU bar ([DESIGN.md:109](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:109)). But the bottom summary still says a single same-night pair and `>0.15`, and says any guard failure discards the arm ([DESIGN.md:235](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:235)). That also contradicts the calibrated rule that a small fidelity exceedance triggers another draw/investigation rather than automatic rejection ([DESIGN.md:125](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:125)).

   Treat lines 237–239 as invalid. Otherwise an operator can keep from one lucky pair or kill a lever on a small, investigable guard excursion.

3. **HIGH — H9 is a global ≥1 GB copy transformation, not an arm targeting only the nine identified copies.**

   `GpuCopyAsyncWrapper` visits every non-fusion computation and wraps every eligible D2D copy above the threshold ([gpu_copy_async_wrapper.cc:79-113](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/backends/gpu/transforms/gpu_copy_async_wrapper.cc#L79-L113)). Thus the nine `copy.7xx` sites and their 1.85–3.22 GB sizes are not an upper bound. Other ≥1 GB copies could dominate memory or attribution.

   For the known sites alone, the async-compute limit of two allows roughly two largest copies—about 6 GB—live concurrently ([gpu_latency_hiding_scheduler.cc:689-718](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_latency_hiding_scheduler.cc#L689-L718)). That is close to the entire slop-85→90 scheduler-budget increase, approximately 6.6 GiB. Although the nominal pool headroom is `138.2 - 116.57 = 21.63 GiB`, the slop-85 scheduler/remat limit already binds; H9 can consume H6’s headroom through longer live ranges without directly exhausting the allocator.

   This fork’s explicit large-buffer scheduler fencing is disabled by default ([debug_options_flags.cc:516-522](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L516-L522)). Rematerialization runs after LHS scheduling, so the existing remat-count log is the right confound detector ([gpu_compiler.cc:2811-2824](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc#L2811-L2824), [gpu_compiler.cc:3298-3325](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc#L3298-L3325)).

   An H9 compile/OOM or remat increase means “negative interaction with this memory budget,” not “async copy is a dead lever.”

4. **HIGH — Current engagement checks do not fully separate no-op, engaged-null, and collateral transformation.**

   For H9, exact target stream placement is useful, but “compute-stream MemcpyD2D → ~0” does not enumerate other copies wrapped by the global pass or prove a useful start/done overlap window. For H10, reduced exposed-copy time can result from changed copy count or compensating waits and does not prove both forward and backward loop rotations.

   Profile-free compile observables should be mandatory:

   - **H9:** dump/count post-`gpu-copy-async-wrapper` `copy-start` sites, byte-size histogram, exact target-site count, total wrapped-site count, and scheduled start→done distance. Record remat count and compiled temp-memory estimate.
   - **H10:** use `TF_CPP_VMODULE=collective_pipeliner=1,hlo_pass_pipeline=3`; record forward/backward transformed-loop and transformed-instruction counts whose `MoveInfo` contains `MoveToHost`/`MoveToDevice`. The pass already logs these counters ([collective_pipeliner.cc:3503-3542](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner.cc#L3503-L3542), [collective_pipeliner.cc:3606-3609](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/collective_pipeliner.cc#L3606-L3609)).
   - Log to W&B summary: exact canonical `XLA_FLAGS`, PJRT version, optimized-HLO fingerprint, transform counts/bytes, start→done schedule distance, remat count, and peak HBM.

   Classification should be:

   - `0` target transforms: predicate-miss/no-op; no dead-lever conclusion.
   - Target transforms plus positive schedule movement/aux-stream placement, MFU below bar: engaged-but-null on this stack.
   - Target transforms plus remat/stall/memory growth: engaged but confounded/negative interaction.

5. **HIGH — Slop-90 cannot enter the control stack mid-comparison without invalidating attribution.**

   H6 directly changes the memory budget H9 consumes, and H9/H10 share async-compute scheduling resources with existing offload operations. The current “keeps become the new control” language ([DESIGN.md:237](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:237)) is insufficient for this interaction.

   Correct ordering:

   - Finish the complete 2C/2T H6 decision before starting H9/H10, or freeze slop-85 until every draw for the current hypothesis finishes.
   - Never pool controls or treatment draws across slop-stack changes.
   - If slop-90 becomes control, prior H9/H10 results remain claims only about slop-85; rerun fresh bracketing controls and treatments on slop-90.
   - Because the H6 budget increase is approximately the H9 two-copy live-range increase, use an H6×H9 2×2 comparison or back-ablate H9 from the final slop-90 stack.
   - If H9 and H10 both keep, the planned joint back-ablation remains mandatory because they share the async-compute limit ([LOGBOOK.md:310](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/LOGBOOK.md:310)).

6. **MEDIUM — H10 can already be active in a nominal control through optimization effort.**

   The exact fork enables host-offload pipelining when the flag is true **or** optimization effort is O1+ ([gpu_compiler.cc:1213-1245](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc#L1213-L1245)). JAX 0.11.1 nominally defaults `jax_optimization_level` to `UNKNOWN` ([config.py:2262](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/jax/_src/config.py:2262)), but the dispatcher forwards arbitrary `JAX_*` environment variables ([dispatch.py:24](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/dispatch.py:24)).

   `JAX_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL` must therefore be asserted unset/`UNKNOWN` in both control and treatment and logged. Otherwise H10 treatment can be an exact control despite the flag being present.

7. **MEDIUM — Exact fork source is verified; exact on-rack binary remains UNVERIFIED.**

   The lock pins the expected wheel URL and SHA256 ([uv.lock:2631](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/uv.lock:2631)), and the fetched fork tag resolves to full commit `c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92`. Both flags are registered there, defaulting to `-1` and `false` respectively ([debug_options_flags.cc:578](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L578), [debug_options_flags.cc:2491](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L2491), [debug_options_flags.cc:3503](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/debug_options_flags.cc#L3503)).

   However, `verify_ragged_pjrt()` accepts any `0.11.1+marin.*` build rather than requiring `c9526e8c0272` ([train.py:256](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:256)). The exact installed on-rack artifact and file hash were not available, so they remain **UNVERIFIED**.

## Per-flag failure surface

| Arm | Compile/startup failure | Silent no-op | Engaged runtime failure |
|---|---|---|---|
| H9 async D2D | Wrong wheel: unknown-flag fatal. Exact wheel: async-HLO creation, verifier, scheduler/remat failure, or compile-time inflation. | No eligible ≥1 GB same-layout D2D copies; generic “pass ran” is insufficient. | Longer live ranges cause extra remat, overlap suppression, fragmentation/OOM, or memory-bandwidth contention. |
| H10 pipelined offload | Wrong wheel: unknown-flag fatal. Exact wheel: CollectivePipeliner/while rewrite or verifier failure. | Predicate miss, unsupported chain/control dependency, or control already enabled by O1+. | Finite silent stale reload, intermittent/rank-local corruption, added peeled/prefetched-buffer memory, or compensating waits. |

Finally, `_apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults` does **not** strip or force either proposed flag. It preserves explicit flags at [train.py:211](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:211); only the collective-overlap name is force-replaced for `offload_carry`, and only the two ragged-required names are replaced at [train.py:238](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:238). Overlap remains correctly pinned to 1.
